\|\sigma^{+}\|^{\mathfrak{C}}=\|\mathsf{sk}(\sigma)\|^{\mathfrak{C}}
: since
\sigma^{+}=\forall\mathcal{X}\mathsf{sk}(\sigma)
, if we let
FV(\sigma^{+})=\{Y_{1},\dots,Y_{n}\}
and
\mathcal{X}=\{X_{1},\dots,X_{n^{\prime}}\}
,
we have

\displaystyle\|\sigma^{+}\|^{\mathfrak{C}}=

\displaystyle~\bigcap_{s_{1},\dots,s_{n}\in S_{\mathfrak{C}}}|\sigma^{+}|^{\mathfrak{C}}(s_{1},\dots,s_{n})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\bigcap_{s_{1},\dots,s_{n}\in S_{\mathfrak{C}}}\left(\bigcap_{t_{1},\dots,t_{n^{\prime}}\in S_{\mathfrak{C}}}|\mathsf{sk}(\sigma)|^{\mathfrak{C}}(s_{1},\dots,s_{n},t_{1},\dots,t_{n^{\prime}})\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\bigcap_{s_{1},\dots,s_{n+n^{\prime}}\in S_{\mathfrak{C}}}|\mathsf{sk}(\sigma)|^{\mathfrak{C}}(s_{1},\dots,s_{n+n^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\|\mathsf{sk}(\sigma)\|^{\mathfrak{C}}.

3.2.
Some concrete realizability semantics

3.2.1.
Stable and saturated sets

Three well-known semantics over
\Lambda
are generated by the following closure operators:

(1)

(\_)^{\beta}:\wp(\Lambda)\to\wp(\Lambda)
given by

s^{\beta}=\{M\in\Lambda\mid\exists M^{\prime}\in s\ M\simeq_{\beta}M^{\prime}\}
;

(2)

(\_)^{\beta\eta}:\wp(\Lambda)\to\wp(\Lambda)
given by

s^{\beta\eta}=\{M\in\Lambda\mid\exists M^{\prime}\in s\ M\simeq_{\beta\eta}M^{\prime}\}
;

(3)

(\_)^{\beta sat}:\wp(\Lambda)\to\wp(\Lambda)
defined inductively as follows:

•

s^{\beta sat}_{0}:=s

•

s^{\beta sat}_{n+1}:=\{(\lambda x.P)QQ_{1}\dots Q_{n}\mid P[Q/x]Q_{1}\dots Q_{n}\in s^{\beta sat}_{n}\}

•

s^{\beta sat}:=\bigcup_{n}s^{\beta sat}_{n}
.

It is easily checked that all three operators above are closure operators over
\Lambda
.
The following is also easily established:

Proposition 31
.

For all
s\subseteq\Lambda
,

(1)

s\in\mathcal{S}_{\beta}
iff
s
is
\beta
-stable
, i.e. for any term
M,N
, if
M\in s
and
M\simeq_{\beta}N
, then
N\in s
.

(2)

s\in\mathcal{S}_{\beta\eta}
iff
s
is
\beta\eta
-stable
, i.e. for any term
M,N
, if
M\in s
and
M\simeq_{\beta\eta}N
, then
N\in s
.

(3)

s\in\mathcal{S}_{\beta sat}
iff
s
is
\beta
-saturated
, i.e.
s
closed with respect to weak-head expansion: for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
and term
M
of the form

(\lambda x.P)QQ_{1}\dots Q_{n}
, if
P[Q/x]Q_{1}\dots Q_{n}\in s
, then
M\in s
.

Proposition 32
.

(\_)^{\beta},(\_)^{\beta\eta}
and
(\_)^{\beta sat}
are
\mathsf{F}
-closure operators. Moreover,

(\_)^{\beta sat}\leq(\_)^{\beta}\leq(\_)^{\beta\eta}
.

Proof 3.15
.

From Proposition
31
, it is clear that
(\_)^{\beta sat}\leq(\_)^{\beta}\leq(\_)^{\beta\eta}
.
We show that
(\_)^{\beta sat}
is a
\mathsf{F}
-closure operator. That
(\_)^{\beta},(\_)^{\beta\eta}
are
\mathsf{F}
-closure operators can be proved in a similar way.

Let
s,t\in\mathcal{S}_{\beta sat}
and suppose
P[Q/x]Q_{1}\dots Q_{n}\in s\to t
. If
s\neq\emptyset
, then for all
R\in s
,

P[Q/x]Q_{1}\dots Q_{n}R\in t
and, since
t\in\mathcal{S}_{\beta sat}
,
(\lambda x.P)QQ_{1}\dots Q_{n}R\in t
. We conclude that for all

R\in s
,
(\lambda x.P)QQ_{1}\dots Q_{n}R\in t
, whence
(\lambda x.P)QQ_{1}\dots Q_{n}\in s\to t
. This shows that
s\to t
is
\beta
-saturated, hence by Proposition
31
,
s\to t\in\mathcal{S}_{\beta sat}
.
If
s=\emptyset
, then
s\to t=\Lambda
and clearly
\Lambda^{\beta sat}=\Lambda
, so again
s\to t\in\mathcal{S}_{\beta sat}
.

The following can be easily verified:

Proposition 33
.

(\_)^{\beta},(\_)^{\beta sat}
and
(\_)^{\beta\eta}
satisfy conditions
\mathsf{SU}
and
\mathsf{K}
.

Since
(\_)^{\beta},(\_)^{\beta sat}
and
(\_)^{\beta\eta}
obviously satisfy condition
\mathsf{R}
, we deduce:

Corollary 34
.

(\_)^{\beta},(\_)^{\beta sat}
and
(\_)^{\beta\eta}
are adequate.

Remark 35
.

It is instructive to consider an example of a non adequate semantics: let
(\_)^{\beta\downarrow}:\wp(\Lambda)\to\wp(\Lambda)
be given by

s^{\beta\downarrow}=\{P\mid\exists M\in s\ M\to^{*}_{\beta}P\}

It can be verified that
(\_)^{\beta\downarrow}
is a
\mathsf{F}
-closure operator. Moreover,
s\in S_{\beta\downarrow}
iff
s
is
\beta
-downward closed
, that is, for any term
M\in s
, if
M\to^{*}_{\beta}M^{\prime}
, then
M^{\prime}\in s
. Now let
s=\{\lambda x.x\}
; then
x[\lambda x.x/x]\in s
, but
\lambda x.x\notin s\to s
, since
(\lambda x.x)\lambda x.x\notin s
.
Hence the semantics
S_{\beta\downarrow}
does not satisfy condition
\mathsf{K}
and is therefore not adequate.

3.2.2.
Reducibility candidates

We define a closure operator
\mathsf{CR}
generating Girard’s reducibility candidates (in an untyped frame, see
[
Gal90
]
) as well as two variants
\mathsf{CR}^{\prime},\mathsf{CR}^{\prime\prime}
which generate variants of reducibility candidates from
[
Cou12
]
. The introduction of these two variants is motivated by the fact that
\mathsf{CR}
is regular, and regularity is an obstacle to investigate completeness and parametricity (see Subsection
4.1
).

We let
\mathsf{CR}:\wp(\mathcal{SN})\to\wp(\mathcal{SN})
be the closure operator defined inductively as follows (see
[
Rib09
]
):

•

\mathsf{CR}_{0}(s)=s^{\beta\downarrow}
(see Remark
35
);

•

\mathsf{CR}_{n+1}(s)=\{M\mid M\in\mathcal{N}\ and\ \forall M^{\prime}\ (M\to_{\beta}M^{\prime}\Rightarrow M^{\prime}\in\mathsf{CR}_{n}(s))\}
;

•

\mathsf{CR}(s)=\bigcup_{n}\mathsf{CR}_{n}(s)
.

That
\mathsf{CR}(s)\in\mathcal{SN}
for all
s\in\mathcal{SN}
is easily checked by induction on
n\in\mathbb{N}
.
We recall the original definition of reducibility candidates.

{defi}

[reducibility candidates,
[
Gir72
,
Gal90
]
]
A set
s
of untyped
\lambda
-terms is a
reducibility candidate
if it satisfies the following conditions:

(
\mathsf{CR}1
):

if
M\in s
, then
M
is strongly normalizing;

(
\mathsf{CR}2
):

if
M\in s
and
M\to_{\beta}^{*}M^{\prime}
, then
M^{\prime}\in s
;

(
\mathsf{CR}3
):

if
M\in\mathcal{N}
and for all
M^{\prime}
such that
M\to_{\beta}M^{\prime}
,
M^{\prime}\in s
, then
M\in s
.

Proposition 36
.

s\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{CR}}
iff
s
is a reducibility candidate.

Proof 3.16
.

If
s
is a reducibility candidate, it is easily verified by induction that for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
\mathsf{CR}_{n}(s)\subseteq s
, whence
\mathsf{CR}(s)\subseteq s
and we conclude
s\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{CR}}
.
Conversely, suppose
s\subseteq\mathcal{SN}
and
s=\mathsf{CR}(s)
. By the assumption
s
satisfies
\mathsf{CR}1
.
Since
\mathsf{CR}_{0}(s)\subseteq\mathsf{CR}(s)\subseteq s
, it follows that
s
satisfies
\mathsf{CR}2
.
Finally, suppose
M\in\mathcal{N}
and for all
M^{\prime}
such that
M\to_{\beta}M^{\prime}
,
M^{\prime}\in s
. Then
M\in\mathsf{CR}_{1}(s)\subseteq\mathsf{CR}(s)\subseteq s
, whence
s
satisfies
\mathsf{CR}3
.

It is well-known that if
s,t
are reducibility candidates, so is
s\to t
(see
[
Gal90
]
). In particular one has that
s\to_{\mathcal{SN}}t=s\to t
. By Proposition
36
this implies that
\mathsf{CR}
is a
\mathsf{F}
-closure operator. Moreover,
\mathsf{CR}
is regular: condition
\mathsf{CR}3
implies that for all
s\subseteq\mathcal{SN}
,
\mathtt{TermVar}\subseteq\mathsf{CR}(s)
. From Proposition
18
we deduce then that
\mathcal{SN}_{0}\in s
, for all reducibility candidate
s
. In other words, for any variable
x
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
and strongly normalizing terms
P_{1},\dots,P_{n}
,
xP_{1}\dots P_{n}\in\mathsf{CR}(s)
, for all
s\subseteq\mathcal{SN}
.

Proposition 37
.

\mathsf{CR}
is an adequate
\mathsf{F}
-closure operator.

Proof 3.17
.

Since
\mathsf{CR}
is regular, by Lemma
22
it suffices to verify that it satisfies condition
\mathsf{K}
. As
\mathsf{CR}
is regular, all
\mathsf{CR}
-closed sets are non-empty.
Let then
s,t\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{CR}}
and
M\in\mathcal{SN}
be such that for all
P\in s
,
M[P/x]\in t
. We argue by induction on
\mathsf{d}(M)+\mathsf{d}(P)
(see Subsection
2.1
) that
(\lambda x.M)P\in t
. If
\mathsf{d}(M)+\mathsf{d}(P)=0
, then
M,P
are
\beta
-normal, hence the unique immediate reduct of
(\lambda x.M)P
is
M[P/x]
, which is in
t
by assumption, hence
(\lambda x.M)P\in t
by
\mathsf{CR}3
.
If
\mathsf{d}(M)+\mathsf{d}(P)>0
, then any immediate reduct of
(\lambda x.M)P
is either
M[P/x]
, which is in
t
by assumption, either of the form

(\lambda x.M^{\prime})P
, with
M\to_{\beta}M^{\prime}
, which is in
t
by the induction hypothesis, or of the form
(\lambda x.M)P^{\prime}
, with
P\to_{\beta}P^{\prime}
, which is again in
t
by the induction hypothesis. Hence all immediate reduct of
(\lambda x.M)P
is in
t
, so
(\lambda x.M)P\in t
by
\mathsf{CR}3
. We can now conclude that
\lambda x.M\in s\to t
.

We now introduce two variants
\mathsf{CR}^{\prime},\mathsf{CR}^{\prime\prime}
of Definition
3.2.2
, corresponding to the variants of reducibility candidates introduced in
[
Cou12
]
. We let
\mathsf{CR}^{\prime},\mathsf{CR}^{\prime\prime}:\wp(\mathcal{SN})\to\wp(\mathcal{SN})
be the closure operators below:

(1)

\mathsf{CR}^{\prime}(s)
is defined inductively as follows:

•

\mathsf{CR}^{\prime}_{0}(s)=s^{\beta\downarrow}
;

•

\mathsf{CR}^{\prime}_{n+1}(s)=\{M\mid M\in\mathcal{N}^{*}\ and\ \forall M^{\prime}\ (M\to_{\beta}M^{\prime}\Rightarrow M^{\prime}\in\mathsf{CR}^{\prime}_{n}(s))\}
;

•

\mathsf{CR}^{\prime}(s)=\bigcup_{n}\mathsf{CR}^{\prime}_{n}(s)
.

(2)

\mathsf{CR}^{\prime\prime}(s)
is defined inductively as follows:

•

\mathsf{CR}^{\prime\prime}_{0}(s)=s^{\beta\downarrow}
;

•

\mathsf{CR}^{\prime\prime}_{n+1}(s)=\{P\mid P=M[F_{1}/x_{1},\dots,F_{n}/x_{n}],F_{1},\dots,F_{n}\in\mathcal{N}^{*}\ \text{and}\ \forall G_{1},\dots,G_{n}\ (F_{1}\to_{\beta}G_{1},\dots,F_{n}\to_{\beta}G_{n}\ \Rightarrow\ M[G_{1}/x_{1},\dots,G_{n}/x_{n}]\in\mathsf{CR}^{\prime\prime}_{n}(s))\}
;

•

\mathsf{CR}^{\prime\prime}(s)=\bigcup_{n}\mathsf{CR}^{\prime\prime}_{n}(s)
.

The corresponding variants of reducibility candidates are obtained by modifying condition
\mathsf{CR}3
.

{defi}

[reducibility candidates, first variant,
[
Cou12
]
]
A set of
\lambda
-terms is a
reducibility candidate
if it satisfies the conditions
\mathsf{CR}1,\mathsf{CR}2
of Definition
3.2.2
plus the condition
\mathsf{CR}3^{\prime}
below:

(
\mathsf{CR}3^{\prime}
):

for all
M\in\mathcal{N}^{*}
, if for all
M^{\prime}
such that
M\to_{\beta}M^{\prime}
,
M^{\prime}\in s
, then
M\in s
.

{defi}

[reducibility candidates, second variant,
[
Cou12
]
]

A set of
\lambda
-terms is a
reducibility candidate
if it satisfies the conditions
\mathsf{CR}1,\mathsf{CR}2
of Definition
3.2.2
plus the condition
\mathsf{CR}3^{\prime\prime}
below:

(
\mathsf{CR}3^{\prime\prime}
):

for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
and terms
M,F_{1},\dots,F_{n}
, if

•:

for all
i\leq n
,
F_{i}\in\mathcal{N}^{*}
;

•:

for all
G_{1},\dots,G_{n}
such that, for all
i=1,\dots,n
,
F_{i}\to_{\beta}G_{i}
,
M[G_{1}/x_{1},\dots,G_{n}/x_{n}]\in s
,

then
M[F_{1}/x_{1},\dots,F_{n}/x_{n}]\in s
.

The following Proposition is proved similarly to Proposition
36
.

Proposition 38
.

(1)

For all
s\subseteq\mathcal{SN}
,
s\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{CR}^{\prime}}
iff
s
is a reducibility candidate according to Definition
3.2.2
.

(2)

For all
s\subseteq\mathcal{SN}
,
s\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{CR}^{\prime\prime}}
iff
s
is a reducibility candidate according to Definition
3.2.2
.

Remark 39
.

The two conditions
\mathsf{CR}3^{\prime}
and
\mathsf{CR}3^{\prime\prime}
correspond to the conditions
\mathsf{CR}3_{aux}
and
\mathsf{CR}3^{\prime}
in
[
Cou12
]
.
We observe that the definition of the two variants of reducibility candidates in
[
Cou12
]
is slightly different as reducibility candidates are there required to be non-empty. This seems problematic for the interpretation of System
\mathsf{F}
, as the resulting family of sets is not closed with respect to arbitrary non-empty intersections.

In
[
Rib07
]
it is proved that Girard’s reducibility candidates are stable by union. By Proposition
21
, this means that
\mathsf{CR}
satisfies
\mathsf{SU}
. The argument presented there can be extended to
\mathsf{CR}^{\prime}
and
\mathsf{CR}^{\prime\prime}
, as we do in detail in Appendix
B
.

Proposition 40
.

\mathsf{CR},\mathsf{CR}^{\prime},\mathsf{CR}^{\prime\prime}
satisfy
\mathsf{SU}
.

Proof 3.18
.

For
\mathsf{CR}
see
[
Rib07
]
, for
\mathsf{CR}^{\prime},\mathsf{CR}^{\prime\prime}
the proof is in Appendix
B
.

Proposition 41
.

\mathsf{CR}^{\prime}
and
\mathsf{CR}^{\prime\prime}
are adequate
\mathsf{F}
-closure operators.

Proof 3.19
.

We only consider the case of
\mathsf{CR}^{\prime\prime}
, as the case of
\mathsf{CR}^{\prime}
can be proved similarly to
\mathsf{CR}
.
Let
s,t\in S_{\mathsf{CR}^{\prime\prime}}
. As we already observed, if
s\neq\emptyset
, then
s\to_{\mathcal{SN}}t=s\to t
. Indeed, for all
P\in s
and
Q\in s\to_{\mathcal{SN}}t
,
QP\in t\subseteq\mathcal{SN}
, and this forces
Q\in\mathcal{SN}
.
We now show that for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
\mathsf{CR}^{\prime\prime}_{n}(s\to t)\subseteq s\to t
. If
n=0
, then let
P\in s\to t
and suppose
P\to_{\beta}^{*}P^{\prime}
. Then for all
Q\in s
,
PQ\to_{\beta}^{*}P^{\prime}Q
, and since
\mathsf{CR}^{\prime\prime}(t)\subseteq t
, we deduce
P^{\prime}Q\in t
. In definitive,
P^{\prime}\in s\to t
, so
\mathsf{CR}_{0}(s\to t)\subseteq s\to t
.
Suppose now
\mathsf{CR}_{n}(s\to t)\subseteq s\to t
and let
P=P_{0}[F_{1}/x_{1},\dots,F_{m}/x_{m}]
for some
m\in\mathbb{N}
and terms
F_{1},\dots,F_{m}\in\mathcal{N}^{*}
such that for all
G_{1},\dots,G_{m}
such that
F_{i}\to_{\beta}G_{i}
for
i=1,\dots,n
,

P_{0}[G_{1}/x_{1},\dots,G_{n}/x_{m}]\in\mathsf{CR}_{n}(s\to t)\subseteq s\to t
. Then for all
Q\in s
, we can choose
y_{1},\dots,y_{m}
not free in
Q
nor in
P_{0}
so that
P=P_{0}[y_{1}/x_{1},\dots,y_{m}/x_{m}][F_{1}/y_{1},\dots,F_{m}/y_{m}]
; let
P^{\prime}_{0}=P_{0}[y_{1}/x_{1},\dots,y_{m}/x_{m}]
. Then
PQ=P^{\prime}_{0}Q[F_{1}/y_{1},\dots,F_{m}/y_{m}]
and for all
G_{1},\dots,G_{m}
such that
F_{i}\to_{\beta}G_{i}
for
i=1,\dots,n
,
P^{\prime}_{0}Q[G_{1}/y_{1},\dots,G_{m}/y_{m}]=P_{0}[G_{1}/x_{1},\dots,G_{m}/x_{m}]Q\in s\to t
by the assumption. Hence, by
\mathsf{CR}3^{\prime\prime}
we deduce
PQ\in\mathsf{CR}^{\prime\prime}(t)\subseteq t
. This shows then that
P\in s\to t
.
Finally, if
s=\emptyset
, then
s\to_{\mathcal{SN}}t=\mathcal{SN}=\mathsf{CR}^{\prime\prime}(\mathcal{SN})
, so again
s\to_{\mathcal{SN}}t
is
\mathsf{CR}^{\prime\prime}
-closed.

It remains to show that
\mathsf{CR}^{\prime\prime}
is adequate. By Propositions
27
and
40
, it suffices to show that
\mathsf{CR}^{\prime\prime}
satisfies condition
\mathsf{K}
. Let
s,t\in S_{\mathsf{CR}^{\prime\prime}}
. If
s\neq\emptyset
, we can argue similarly to the case of
\mathsf{CR}
.
If
s=\emptyset
, we must show that for all
P\in\mathcal{SN}
,
\lambda x.P\in s\to_{\mathcal{SN}}t
, but this follows from the fact that

s\to_{\mathcal{SN}}t=\Lambda\cap\mathcal{SN}=\mathcal{SN}
.

Observe that we have
\mathsf{CR}^{\prime}\leq{\mathsf{CR}^{\prime\prime}}
, since condition
\mathsf{CR}3^{\prime}
is a particular case of condition
\mathsf{CR}3^{\prime\prime}
. Moreover, since
\mathsf{CR}^{\prime\prime}(s)\subseteq s^{\beta}
, we have
{\mathsf{CR}^{\prime\prime}}\leq(\_)^{\beta}
.
In definitive we have
(\_)^{\beta\downarrow}\leq{\mathsf{CR}^{\prime}}\leq{\mathsf{CR}^{\prime\prime}}\leq(\_)^{\beta}
. In Appendix
B
it is also shown that
(\_)^{\beta sat}\leq{\mathsf{CR}^{\prime}},{\mathsf{CR}^{\prime\prime}}
(Corollary
98
), that is, that the semantics
\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{CR}^{\prime}},\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{CR}^{\prime\prime}}
are closed with respect to strongly normalizing weak head expansion.

Finally, one can consider closure operators
\mathsf{CR}_{\eta},\mathsf{CR}^{\prime}_{\eta},\mathsf{CR}^{\prime\prime}_{\eta}
obtained by replacing
\beta
-reduction with
\beta\eta
-reduction, yielding in a similar way semantics of
\beta\eta
-reducibility candidates (see
[
Gal90
]
).

3.2.3.
Properties of closure operators

We introduce some technical properties of closure operator that will be used in the next sections.

{defi}

Let
L\subseteq\Lambda
and
\mathfrak{C}
be a closure operator over
L
. We define the properties of
stability by application
(
\mathsf{SA}
) and
stability by substitution
(
\mathsf{SS}
) as follows:

(
\mathsf{SA}
):

for all
P,P^{\prime},Q\in L
, if
P^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{C}(\{P\})
and
PQ\in L
, then
P^{\prime}Q\in\mathfrak{C}(\{PQ\})
;

(
\mathsf{SS}
):

for all
P,P^{\prime},Q_{1},\dots,Q_{n}\in L
, if
P^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{C}(\{P\})
and
P[Q_{1}/x_{1},\dots,Q_{n}/x_{n}]\in L
, then

P^{\prime}[Q_{1}/x_{1},\dots,Q_{n}/x_{n}]\in\mathfrak{C}(\{P[Q_{1}/x_{1},\dots,Q_{n}/x_{n}]\})
.

\mathsf{SA}
is satisfied by all closure operators defined in the previous subsection and will be exploited in Section
5
to define closed logical relations.

Proposition 42
.

(\_)^{\beta},(\_)^{\beta\eta},(\_)^{\beta sat}
satisfy
\mathsf{SA}
.

Proof 3.20
.

If
P^{\prime}\simeq_{\beta}P
, then
P^{\prime}Q\simeq_{\beta}PQ
, whence
P^{\prime}Q\in\{PQ\}^{\beta}
. This shows that
(\_)^{\beta}
satisfies
\mathsf{SA}
. A similar argument holds for
(\_)^{\beta\eta}
.

If
P^{\prime}\in\{P\}^{\beta sat}
, then

P^{\prime}\to_{wh}P
. Now, for all
Q
,
P^{\prime}Q\to_{wh}PQ
, whence
P^{\prime}Q\in\{PQ\}^{\beta sat}
. This shows that
(\_)^{\beta sat}
satisfies
\mathsf{SA}
.

Proposition 43
.

\mathsf{CR},\mathsf{CR}^{\prime},\mathsf{CR}^{\prime\prime}
satisfy
\mathsf{SA}
.

Proof 3.21
.

We consider the case of
\mathsf{CR}
only, as the other two cases are proved in a similar way.
Let
P,P^{\prime},Q\in\mathcal{SN}
, where
P^{\prime}\in\mathsf{CR}(\{P\})
and suppose
PQ\in\mathcal{SN}
.
We show that
P^{\prime}Q\in\mathcal{SN}
and
P^{\prime}Q\in\mathsf{CR}(\{P\})
by induction on
\mathsf{c}(P^{\prime})+\mathsf{d}(Q)
, where
\mathsf{c}
is the least
n
such that
P^{\prime}\in\mathsf{CR}_{n}(\{P\})
and
\mathsf{d}
was defined in Subsection
2.1
.
If
\mathsf{c}(P^{\prime})+\mathsf{d}(Q)=0
, then
P\to_{\beta}^{*}P^{\prime}
. Hence
PQ\to_{\beta}^{*}P^{\prime}Q
, whence
P^{\prime}Q\in\mathsf{CR}_{0}(\{PQ\})\subseteq\mathsf{CR}(\{PQ\})
.
Suppose
\mathsf{c}(P^{\prime})+\mathsf{d}(Q)=k+1
. If
\mathsf{c}(P^{\prime})=0
we can argue as above. Otherwise it must be
P^{\prime}\in\mathcal{N}
. Hence any immediate reduct of
P^{\prime}Q
is either of the form
P^{\prime\prime}Q
, where
P^{\prime\prime}
is an immediate reduct of
P^{\prime}
or of the form
P^{\prime}Q^{\prime}
, where
Q^{\prime}
is an immediate reduct of
Q
. In both cases, we deduce
P^{\prime\prime}Q\in\mathsf{CR}(\{P\})
by the induction hypothesis. Hence all immediate reducts of
P^{\prime}Q
are in
\mathsf{CR}(\{PQ\})
and we conclude
P^{\prime}Q\in\mathsf{CR}(\{PQ\})
by
\mathsf{CR}3
.

\mathsf{SS}
can be easily established for
(\_)^{\beta},(\_)^{\beta sat}
and
(\_)^{\beta\eta}
.

Proposition 44
.

(\_)^{\beta},(\_)^{\beta sat}
and
(\_)^{\beta\eta}
satisfy
\mathsf{SS}
.

Proof 3.22
.

The unique non-trivial case is
(\_)^{\beta sat}
, where
\mathsf{SS}
follows from the fact that if
P^{\prime}\to_{wh}^{*}P
, then
P^{\prime}[Q/x]\to_{wh}^{*}P[Q/x]
.

Unlike
\mathsf{SU}
and
\mathsf{SA}
,
\mathsf{SS}
is not satisfied by reducibility candidates and its variants.
We show in Appendix
B
that
\mathsf{SS}
fails for
{\mathsf{CR}^{\prime}}
(Corollary
96
), the argument being easily adapted to
\mathsf{CR}
and
\mathsf{CR}^{\prime\prime}
.

4.
Completeness for positive types

The completeness problem for the semantics generated by a closure operator
\mathfrak{C}
and a class of types
\mathcal{C}
is the one to know whether for all
\sigma\in\mathcal{C}
and closed (normal) term
M
, if
M\in|\sigma|^{\mathfrak{C}}
, then
M
has type
\sigma
.
Several completeness results for positive types are known from the literature for the semantics discussed in the previous section. We list the main ones below:

Theorem 45

(
[
Hin83a
,
LS86
,
FN98
]
)
.

Let
M
be a closed
\lambda
-term and
\sigma
a type.

(1)

if
\sigma\in\mathtt{T}_{0}
and
M\in\|\sigma\|^{\beta}
, then for some
M^{\prime}\simeq_{\beta}M
,
\vdash M^{\prime}:\sigma
;

(2)

if
\sigma\in\forall^{+}
and
M\in\|\sigma\|^{\beta\eta}
, then for some
M^{\prime}\simeq_{\beta\eta}M
,
\vdash M^{\prime}:\sigma
;

(3)

if
\sigma\in\forall^{+}_{\mathtt{P}}
and
M\in\|\sigma\|^{\beta sat}
, then for some
M^{\prime}\simeq_{\beta}M
,
\vdash M^{\prime}:\sigma
.

Remark 46
.

Claim
1.
can be extended to
\forall^{+}_{\mathtt{P}}
types by means of Theorem
14
and Proposition
30
:
if
\sigma\in\forall^{+}
and
M\in\|\sigma\|^{\beta}
, then by Proposition
30
,
M\in\|\sigma^{+}\|^{\beta}=\|\mathsf{sk}(\sigma)\|^{\beta}
, hence by
1.
, for some
M^{\prime}\simeq_{\beta}M
,
\vdash M^{\prime}:\mathsf{sk}(\sigma)
; finally, by Theorem
14
, we get
\vdash M^{\prime}:\sigma
.

Claims 1. and 3. can be strengthened in the case of closed
\beta
-normal
\lambda
-terms, as a consequence of subject
\beta
-reduction. For instance, if
\sigma\in\mathtt{T}_{0}
and
M\in\|\sigma\|^{\beta}
is closed
\beta
-normal, then
\vdash M:\sigma
. Indeed, from claim 1., for some
M^{\prime}\simeq_{\beta}M
,
\vdash M^{\prime}:\sigma
, and, since it must be
M^{\prime}\to_{\beta}^{*}M
, we deduce
\vdash M:\sigma
by subject
\beta
-reduction.

Finally, claim 2. can be strengthened in the case of closed
\beta\eta
-normal
\lambda
-terms, as a consequence of subject
\beta
-reduction. If
\sigma\in\mathtt{T}_{0}
and
M\in\|\sigma\|^{\beta\eta}
is closed
\beta\eta
-normal, then for some
M^{\prime\prime}
such that
M^{\prime\prime}\to_{\eta}^{*}M
,
\vdash M^{\prime\prime}:\sigma
. Indeed, from Theorem
45
, for some
M^{\prime}\simeq_{\beta\eta}M
,
\vdash M^{\prime}:\sigma
, hence, since it must be
M^{\prime}\to_{\beta\eta}^{*}M
, by subject
\beta
-reduction we deduce
\vdash M^{\prime\prime}:\sigma
, where
M^{\prime\prime}
is the
\beta
-normal form of
M^{\prime\prime}
.

The completeness properties in Theorem
45
are proved by a similar technique, which consists in constructing an interpretation made of typable terms.
The aim of this section is to discuss the conditions under which this technique can be applied to prove completeness for an arbitrary semantics generated by a
\mathsf{F}
-closure operator.
In particular, we focus on a property, that we call
\mathsf{F}
-adaptedness (inspired from
[
Cou12
]
), which plays a central role in such arguments.

By relying on this property, and on the syntactic analysis of positive types in Section
2
, we reconstruct the completeness arguments for positive types in the more general framework introduced in Section
3
.

4.1.
\mathsf{F}
-adapted closure operators

The proofs of the completeness results mentioned above are based on the construction of a “term model”, that is, an interpretation made of sets of typable terms. The construction is based on the notion of infinite context that we recall below:

{defi}

Let
(\tau_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
be an enumeration of all types such that for any type
\sigma
, the set
\{\tau_{i}\mid\tau_{i}=\sigma\}
is infinite. We let
\Gamma^{\infty}=\{x_{i}:\tau_{i}\mid i\in\mathbb{N}\}
be an infinite set of type declarations. For any
M\in\Lambda
and
\sigma\in\mathtt{T}
, we let
\Gamma^{\infty}\vdash M:\sigma
indicate the judgement
\Gamma^{\infty}_{M}\vdash M:\sigma
, where
\Gamma^{\infty}_{M}
is the context
\{x_{i_{1}}:\tau_{i_{1}},\dots,x_{i_{n}}:\tau_{i_{n}}\}
, for
\mathtt{FV}(M)=\{x_{i_{1}},\dots,x_{i_{n}}\}
.

For any type
\sigma
, we let
\sigma^{\infty}=\{M\mid\Gamma^{\infty}\vdash M:\sigma\}
.

Given a closure operator
\mathfrak{C}
, the sets
\sigma^{\infty}
need not be
\mathfrak{C}
-closed. For instance, the sets
\sigma^{\infty}
are not
\beta
-stable, as typability in System
\mathsf{F}
is not closed with respect to
\beta
-equivalence.
A
\mathfrak{C}
-interpretation is obtained as follows:

{defi}

Given an adequate
\mathsf{F}
-closure operator
\mathfrak{C}
, we let
\mathcal{M}^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{C}}
be the
\mathfrak{C}
-interpretation given by

\mathcal{M}^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{C}}(X)=\mathfrak{C}(X^{\infty})
.

To establish completeness one has to show that the sets

|\sigma|^{\mathfrak{C}}_{\mathcal{M}^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{C}}}
and
\mathfrak{C}(\sigma^{\infty})
coincide when
\sigma
is a positive type.
The property below is an essential ingredient to prove this fact.

{defi}

[
\mathsf{F}
-adapted]
Let
\mathfrak{C}
be a
\mathsf{F}
-closure operator.
\mathfrak{C}
is
\mathsf{F}
-adapted
if for all types
\sigma,\tau\in\mathtt{T}
,

\mathfrak{C}((\sigma\to\tau)^{\infty})\ =\ \mathfrak{C}(\sigma^{\infty})\to\mathfrak{C}(\tau^{\infty})

(8)

In other words, a
\mathsf{F}
-closure operator
\mathfrak{C}
is
\mathsf{F}
-adapted when for all types
\sigma,\tau
and term
M
,
the two properties below are equivalent:

•

for all
P
in the
\mathfrak{C}
-closure of terms of type
\sigma
,
MP
is in the
\mathfrak{C}
-closure of the terms of type
\tau
;

•

M
is in the
\mathfrak{C}
-closure of the terms of type
\sigma\to\tau
.

For any
\mathsf{F}
-adapted
\mathsf{F}
-closure operator one can easily prove that
|\sigma|^{\mathfrak{C}}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{C}}^{\infty}}=\mathfrak{C}(\sigma^{\infty})
holds for simple types:

Proposition 47
.

Let
\mathfrak{C}
be a
\mathsf{F}
-adapted
\mathsf{F}
-closure operator. Then for all simple type
\sigma\in\mathtt{T}_{0}

|\sigma|^{{\mathfrak{C}}}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{C}}^{\infty}}=\mathfrak{C}(\sigma^{\infty})
.

Proof 4.1
.

We argue by induction on
\sigma
. If
\sigma=X
, then the claim holds by definition. If
\sigma=\sigma_{1}\to\sigma_{2}
, then by induction hypothesis
|\sigma_{i}|^{\mathfrak{C}}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{C}}^{\infty}}=\mathfrak{C}(\sigma_{i}^{\infty})
, for
i=1,2
. Then

|\sigma|^{\mathfrak{C}}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{C}}^{\infty}}=|\sigma_{1}|^{\mathfrak{C}}_{\mathcal{M}^{\infty}}\to|\sigma_{2}|^{\mathfrak{C}}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathfrak{C}}^{\infty}}=\mathfrak{C}(\sigma_{1}^{\infty})\to\mathfrak{C}(\sigma_{2}^{\infty})=\mathfrak{C}(\sigma^{\infty})
, since
\mathfrak{C}
is
\mathsf{F}
-adapted.

Definition
4.1
is inspired from some remarks in
[
Cou12
]
. There the introduction of the second variant
\mathsf{CR}^{\prime\prime}
of reducibility candidates is motivated by the need to obtain a semantics satisfying Equation
8
(that is,
\mathsf{F}
-adapted). Indeed the following results hold:

Proposition 48
.

(i)

\mathsf{CR}^{\prime\prime}
is
\mathsf{F}
-adapted.

(ii)

\mathsf{CR}
and
\mathsf{CR}^{\prime}
are not
\mathsf{F}
-adapted.

Proof 4.2
.

Claim (i) is Lemma 4.10, p. 20 in
[
Cou12
]
. For Claim (ii),
let
\tau
be a simple type and
x
a variable such that
x:\tau\in\Gamma^{\infty}
. Let
P
be a closed strongly normalizing term which cannot have type
\tau
(e.g.
P=\lambda z.zz
), and
let
M=(\lambda y.x)P
.
M
is neutral and not
\beta
-normal, and
M\to_{\beta}x
. Then
x\in\tau^{\infty}
, whence
M\in\mathsf{CR}(\tau^{\infty})
. Morever, we have
\lambda x^{\prime}.M\in\mathsf{CR}(\tau^{\infty})\to\mathsf{CR}(\tau^{\infty})
, where
x^{\prime}
is a new variable. However,
\lambda x^{\prime}.M\notin\mathsf{CR}((\tau\to\tau)^{\infty})
:
\Gamma^{\infty}\vdash\lambda x^{\prime}.M:\tau\to\tau
is not derivable as
P
cannot have type
\tau
, and for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
\lambda x^{\prime}.M\notin\mathsf{CR}_{n}((\tau\to\tau)^{\infty})
as
\lambda x^{\prime}.M
is not neutral.
A similar argument can be made for
\mathsf{CR}^{\prime}
.

We conclude this subsection by proving that
(\_)^{\beta}
and
(\_)^{\beta\eta}
are
\mathsf{F}
-adapted. The arguments below follow the completeness proofs in
[
Hin83a
]
and
[
FN98
]
.

Proposition 49
.

(\_)^{\beta\eta}
is
\mathsf{F}
-adapted.

Proof 4.3
.

Let
\sigma=\tau\to\rho
,
P\in(\tau^{\infty})^{\beta\eta}\to(\rho^{\infty})^{\beta\eta}
and let
x_{i}
be a variable not occurring free in
P
and such that
\tau_{i}=\tau
. From
x_{i}:\tau\vdash x_{i}:\tau
, we deduce
x_{i}\in(\tau^{\infty})^{\beta\eta}
and we conclude that
Px_{i}\in(\rho^{\infty})^{\beta\eta}
. Hence for some
Q\simeq_{\beta\eta}Px_{i}
,
\Gamma^{\infty}\vdash Q:\rho
holds, whence
\Gamma^{\infty}\vdash\lambda x_{i}.Q:\sigma
and from
\lambda x_{i}.Q\simeq_{\beta\eta}\lambda x_{i}.Px_{i}\simeq_{\eta}P
we deduce
P\in(\sigma^{\infty})^{\beta\eta}
.

For the converse direction suppose
P\in(\sigma^{\infty})^{\beta\eta}
, hence for some
P^{\prime}\simeq_{\beta\eta}P
,
\Gamma^{\infty}\vdash P^{\prime}:\sigma
holds. Now
let
Q\in(\tau^{\infty})^{\beta\eta}
, hence for some
Q^{\prime}\simeq_{\beta\eta}Q
,
\Gamma^{\infty}\vdash Q^{\prime}:\tau
holds.
We have then
\Gamma^{\infty}\vdash P^{\prime}Q^{\prime}:\rho
, whence
PQ\simeq_{\beta\eta}P^{\prime}Q^{\prime}\in(\rho^{\infty})^{\beta\eta}
. We conclude that

P\in(\tau^{\infty})^{\beta\eta}\to(\rho^{\infty})^{\beta\eta}
.

Proposition 50
.

(\_)^{\beta}
is
\mathsf{F}
-adapted.

Proof 4.4
.

Let
\sigma=\tau\to\rho
,
P\in(\tau^{\infty})^{\beta}\to(\rho^{\infty})^{\beta}
and let
x_{i}
be a variable not occurring free in
P
and such that
\tau_{i}=\tau
. From
x_{i}:\tau\vdash x_{i}:\tau
, we deduce
x_{i}\in(\tau^{\infty})^{\beta}
and we conclude that
Px_{i}\in(\rho^{\infty})^{\beta}
.
Hence for some
Q\simeq_{\beta}Px_{i}
,
\Gamma^{\infty}\vdash Q:\rho
holds. As
Q
is simply typable, it is strongly normalizable, and from
Px_{i}\simeq_{\beta}Q
it follows that
Px_{i}
has a
\beta
-normal form
P^{\prime}
(that of
Q
).
Moreover, as
Q\to^{*}_{\beta}P^{\prime}
, we have
\Gamma^{\infty}\vdash P^{\prime}:\rho
.
We consider now two cases:

1.

for all terms
Q
such that
P\to_{\beta}^{*}Q
,
Q
is neutral. Then
P^{\prime}
is neutral and of the form
x_{j}P_{1}\dots P_{n}x_{i}
where
P\to_{\beta}^{*}x_{j}P_{1}\dots P_{n}
. Now from
\Gamma^{\infty}\vdash P^{\prime}:\rho
it follows that
\Gamma^{\infty}\vdash x_{j}P_{1}\dots P_{n}:\tau\to\rho
. Since
P\simeq_{\beta}x_{j}P_{1}\dots P_{n}
, we deduce
P\in(\sigma^{\infty})^{\beta}
.

2.

The normal form of
P
(which exists since
Px_{i}
has a normal form) is of the form
Q=\lambda y.Q^{\prime}
. Hence
P^{\prime}=Q^{\prime}[x_{i}/y]
and from
\Gamma^{\infty}\vdash P^{\prime}:\rho
we deduce
\Gamma^{\infty}\vdash\lambda x_{i}.P^{\prime}:\sigma
. Now
\lambda x_{i}.P^{\prime}
and
\lambda y.Q^{\prime}
are the same term, so we conclude
P\in(\sigma^{\infty})^{\beta\eta}
.

In both cases we showed then that
P\in(\sigma^{\infty})^{\beta}
.

For the converse direction suppose
P\in(\sigma^{\infty})^{\beta}
, hence for some
P^{\prime}\simeq_{\beta}P
,
\Gamma^{\infty}\vdash P^{\prime}:\sigma
holds. Now
let
Q\in(\tau^{\infty})^{\beta}
, hence for some
Q^{\prime}\simeq_{\beta}Q
,
\Gamma^{\infty}\vdash Q^{\prime}:\tau
holds.
We have then
\Gamma^{\infty}\vdash P^{\prime}Q^{\prime}:\rho
, whence
PQ\simeq_{\beta}P^{\prime}Q^{\prime}\in(\rho^{\infty})^{\beta}
. We conclude that

P\in(\tau^{\infty})^{\beta}\to(\rho^{\infty})^{\beta}
.

It is worth stressing that, unlike Proposition
49
, Proposition
50
exploits the fact that
\lambda_{\to}
is strongly normalizing, as well as the subject
\beta
-reduction property.

Remark 51
.

When
\sigma
is not a positive type, the
\mathfrak{C}
-closed sets
|\sigma|^{\mathfrak{C}}_{\mathcal{M}^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{C}}}
and
\mathfrak{C}(\sigma^{\infty})
can be very different, even for well-behaved closure operators as
\mathfrak{C}=\beta,\beta\eta
.
For instance, let
\mathfrak{C}=(\_)^{\beta}
and
\sigma=\mathbf{Nat}\to\mathbf{Nat}
, where
\mathbf{Nat}=\forall X((X\to X)\to(X\to X))
. Then
\mathfrak{C}(\sigma^{\infty})
contains all terms
\beta
-equivalent to a term
M
such that
\Gamma^{\infty}\vdash M:\sigma
. It can be easily verified that
|\sigma|^{\mathfrak{C}}
contains all terms
M
coding total unary recursive functions, and from a well-known incompleteness argument (see
[
GLT89
]
), such terms cannot all belong to
\mathfrak{C}(\sigma^{\infty})
.

4.2.
Completeness results

We reconstruct the completeness arguments for positive types, by exploiting the analysis of the previous subsection of
\mathsf{F}
-adapted closure operators. Our result allows one to show completeness also for the semantics generated by a non
\mathsf{F}
-adapted closure operator, as soon as this is finer than
(\_)^{\beta}
or
(\_)^{\beta\eta}
(this is the case of
\mathsf{CR}^{\prime}
, for instance).

Our treatment does not cover the semantics generated by a regular
\mathsf{F}
-closure operator (hence, in particular, Girard’s reducibility candidates). It seems that a different method has to be looked for in this case (we shortly discuss this point in the concluding section).

Theorem 52

(
\forall^{+}
-completeness for
\mathfrak{C}\leq(\_)^{\beta\eta}
)
.

Let
\mathfrak{C}
be an adequate
\mathsf{F}
-closure operator over
L\subseteq\Lambda
such that
L
is
\beta\eta
-closed and

\mathfrak{C}\leq(\_)^{\beta\eta}
. Let
M
be a closed
\lambda
-term and
\sigma\in\forall^{+}
. If
M\in\|\sigma\|^{\mathfrak{C}}
, then there exists
M^{\prime}\simeq_{\beta\eta}M
such that
\vdash M^{\prime}:\sigma
.

Proof 4.5
.

Let
M
be a closed term,
\sigma\in\forall^{+}
and suppose
M\in\|\sigma\|^{\mathfrak{C}}
. From Lemma
30
1. we deduce
M\in\|\sigma^{+}\|^{\mathfrak{C}}=\|\mathsf{sk}(\sigma)\|^{\mathfrak{C}}
.
From Theorem
28
it follows that, for all type variable
X_{i}
, if
\Gamma^{\infty}\vdash t:X_{i}
holds, then

t\in|X_{i}|^{\mathfrak{C}}_{\mathcal{M}^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{C}}}=\mathfrak{C}(X_{i}^{\infty})
. This implies in particular that
X^{\infty}\subseteq\mathfrak{C}(X_{i}^{\infty})\subseteq L
. Moreover, since
L
is
\beta\eta
-closed, for all
s\subseteq L
,
s^{\beta\eta}\cap L=s^{\beta\eta}\in S_{\mathfrak{C}}
by Proposition
17
.
Hence, since
\mathfrak{C}\leq(\_)^{\beta\eta}
, the sets
(X_{i}^{\infty})^{\beta\eta}\cap L=(X_{i}^{\infty})^{\beta\eta}
are
\mathfrak{C}
-closed.
Therefore, if we let
s_{i}:=(X_{i}^{\infty})^{\beta\eta}
,
M\in|\mathsf{sk}(\sigma)|^{\mathfrak{C}}(\vec{s})=|\mathsf{sk}(\sigma)|^{\mathfrak{C}}_{\mathcal{M}^{\infty}_{\beta\eta}}
. By Proposition
49

(\_)^{\beta\eta}
is
\mathsf{F}
-adapted, hence by Proposition
47
,

M\in(\mathsf{sk}(\sigma)^{\infty})^{\beta\eta}
, that is, there is
M^{\prime}\simeq_{\beta\eta}M
such that
\vdash M^{\prime}:\mathsf{sk}(\sigma)
. As
M^{\prime}
is closed, by Proposition
14
, we conclude that there exists
M^{\prime\prime}\simeq_{\eta}M^{\prime}\simeq_{\beta\eta}M
such that
\vdash M^{\prime\prime}:\sigma
.

Theorem 53

(
\forall^{+}_{\mathtt{P}}
-completeness for
\mathfrak{C}\leq(\_)^{\beta}
)
.

Let
\mathfrak{C}
be an adequate
\mathsf{F}
-closure operator over
L\subseteq\Lambda
such that
L
is
\beta
-closed and
\mathfrak{C}\leq(\_)^{\beta}
. Let
M
be a closed
\lambda
-term and
\sigma\in\forall^{+}_{\mathtt{P}}
. If
M\in\|\sigma\|^{\mathfrak{C}}
, then there exists
M^{\prime}\simeq_{\beta}M
such that
\vdash M^{\prime}:\sigma
.

Proof 4.6
.

One can argue similarly to Theorem
52
.

By arguing as in Remark
46
we deduce the following:

Corollary 54
.

Let
\mathfrak{C}
be an adequate
\mathsf{F}
-closure operator over a
\beta\eta
-closed set
L\subseteq\Lambda
.

(i)

Suppose
\mathfrak{C}\leq(\_)^{\beta\eta}
. Let
M
be a
\beta\eta
-normal closed
\lambda
-term and
\sigma\in\forall^{+}
. If
M\in\|\sigma\|^{\mathfrak{C}}
, then there exists
M^{\prime}\to_{\eta}^{*}M
such that
\vdash M^{\prime}:\sigma
.

(ii)

Suppose
\mathfrak{C}\leq(\_)^{\beta}
. Let
M
be a
\beta
-normal closed
\lambda
-term and
\sigma\in\forall^{+}_{\mathtt{P}}
. If
M\in\|\sigma\|^{\mathfrak{C}}
, then
\vdash M:\sigma
.

Corollary
54
(i) applies to
(\_)^{\beta\eta}
,
{\mathsf{CR}^{\prime}_{\beta\eta}}
,
{\mathsf{CR}^{\prime\prime}_{\beta\eta}}
, while
Corollary
54
(ii) applies to
(\_)^{\beta}
,
(\_)^{\beta sat}
,
{\mathsf{CR}^{\prime}}
,
{\mathsf{CR}^{\prime\prime}}
.
Finally, from Proposition
48
and some properties of
\mathsf{CR}^{\prime\prime}
proved in Appendix
B
, we deduce that
\mathsf{CR}^{\prime\prime}
satisfies a slightly stronger form of completeness:

Theorem 55

(
\forall^{+}
-completeness of
{\mathsf{CR}^{\prime\prime}}
)
.

Let
M
be a closed
\lambda
-term and
\sigma\in\forall^{+}
. If
M\in\|\sigma\|^{{\mathsf{CR}^{\prime\prime}}}
, then there exists
M^{\prime}
such that
M\in\mathsf{CR}^{\prime\prime}(\{M^{\prime}\})
and
\vdash M^{\prime}:\sigma
.

Proof 4.7
.

Let
M
be closed,
\sigma\in\forall^{+}
, and suppose
M\in\|\sigma\|^{\mathsf{CR}^{\prime\prime}}
. From Lemma
30
1. we deduce
M\in\|\sigma^{+}\|^{\mathsf{CR}^{\prime\prime}}=\|\mathsf{sk}(\sigma)\|^{\mathsf{CR}^{\prime\prime}}
. Let, for all
i\in\mathbb{N}
,
s_{i}:=\mathsf{CR}^{\prime\prime}(X_{i}^{\infty})
; then
M\in|\mathsf{sk}(\sigma)|^{\mathsf{CR}^{\prime\prime}}(\vec{s})=|\mathsf{sk}(\sigma)|^{\mathsf{CR}^{\prime\prime}}_{\mathcal{M}^{\infty}}
. By Proposition
48
and Proposition
47
we deduce

M\in\mathsf{CR}^{\prime\prime}(\mathsf{sk}(\sigma)^{\infty})
.
Now, since
{\mathsf{CR}^{\prime\prime}}
satisfies
\mathsf{SU}
,
M\in\mathsf{CR}^{\prime\prime}(\mathsf{sk}(\sigma)^{\infty})
implies that for some
M^{\prime}\in\mathsf{sk}(\sigma)^{\infty}
,
M\in\mathsf{CR}^{\prime\prime}(\{M^{\prime}\})
.

5.
Closure operators and logical relations

We formalise logical relations in the semantics generated by a
\mathsf{F}
-closure operator. We will suppose that the closure operator satisfies both
\mathsf{SU}
and
\mathsf{SA}
. When
\mathsf{SU}
holds, closed sets form a topology and closed logical relations satisfy then the universal property of the product topology (Proposition
58
). When
\mathsf{SA}
holds, closed logical relations can be shown to be closed with respect to System
\mathsf{F}
connectives.

We define a notion of parametricity as invariance with respect to
\mathfrak{C}
-closed logical relations (
\mathfrak{C}
-invariance
). We establish within this framework the standard result that typable terms are
\mathfrak{C}
-invariant.
Since, by the completeness results of last section, for positive types, closed normal
\mathfrak{C}
-interpretable
\lambda
-terms are typable, we can deduce that such terms are also
\mathfrak{C}
-invariant.
Moreover, if
\mathfrak{C}
is stable by substitutions, we prove that
\mathfrak{C}
-interpretability implies
\mathfrak{C}
-invariance at any type (Theorem
71
). This implies in particular that the closed realizers at any type for the semantics described in the last section are invariant with respect to
\beta
and
\beta\eta
-stable logical relations.

Finally, we shortly discuss Reynolds’ parametricity: for simple types it coincides with invariance with respect to logical relations, while for second order types it provides a stronger condition which requires an extension of the equational theory of System
\mathsf{F}
terms.

5.1.
Logical relations in realizability semantics

5.1.1.
Closure operators via the product topology

If
\mathfrak{C}
is stable by union, the extension of a closure operator over sets of
\lambda
-terms into a closure operator over relations between
\lambda
-terms has a natural topological counterpart. Indeed, as mentioned in Section
3
, if a closure operator
\mathfrak{C}
over
L\subseteq\Lambda
satisfies
\mathsf{SU}
, the family
\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{C}}\cup\{\emptyset\}
is a topology over
L
having the
\mathfrak{C}
-closed sets plus
\emptyset
as open sets. One can define then the
product topology
over
L\times L
, which has as basis
the sets of the form
s\times t
, for
s,t

\mathfrak{C}
-closed, plus
\emptyset
.

Observe that if
\mathfrak{C}
is stable by union, the topology
\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{C}}\cup\{\emptyset\}
has as basis
\mathcal{B}=\{\mathfrak{C}(\{M\})\mid M\in L\}\cup\{\emptyset\}
. Whence the product topology over
L\times L
has as basis the sets of the form

\mathfrak{C}(\{P\})\times\mathfrak{C}(\{Q\})
plus
\emptyset
.

This topological intuition leads to the following definition:

{defi}

Let
\mathfrak{C}_{1}
and
\mathfrak{C}_{2}
be closure operators over two sets
L_{1},L_{2}
, respectively. Then the map
\mathfrak{C}_{1}\times\mathfrak{C}_{2}:\wp(L_{1}\times L_{2})\to\wp(L_{1}\times L_{2})
is defined as follows:

•

(\mathfrak{C}_{1}\times\mathfrak{C}_{2})(\emptyset)=\mathfrak{C}_{1}(\emptyset)\times\mathfrak{C}_{2}(\emptyset)
;

•

if
r\neq\emptyset
,
(\mathfrak{C}_{1}\times\mathfrak{C}_{2})(r)=\{(x,y)\mid\exists(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})\in r\ \text{s.t.}\ x\in\mathfrak{C}_{1}(\{x^{\prime}\}),y\in\mathfrak{C}_{2}(\{y^{\prime}\})\}
.

The proposition below shows that
(\mathfrak{C}_{1}\times\mathfrak{C}_{2})
is a closure operator:

Proposition 56
.

If
\mathfrak{C}_{1},\mathfrak{C}_{2}
are closure operators over
L_{1},L_{2}
, then

\mathfrak{C}_{1}\times\mathfrak{C}_{2}
is a closure operator over
L_{1}\times L_{2}
, that is for all
r\subseteq L_{1}\times L_{2}
the following hold:

(1)

r\subseteq(\mathfrak{C}_{1}\times\mathfrak{C}_{2})(r)
;

(2)

r\subseteq r^{\prime}\Rightarrow(\mathfrak{C}_{1}\times\mathfrak{C}_{2})(r)\subseteq(\mathfrak{C}_{1}\times\mathfrak{C}_{2})(r^{\prime})
;

(3)

(\mathfrak{C}_{1}\times\mathfrak{C}_{2})^{2}(r)\subseteq(\mathfrak{C}_{1}\times\mathfrak{C}_{2})(r)
.

Proof 5.1
.

We first consider the case
r\neq\emptyset
.
Property
1.
follows from the definition of
\mathfrak{C}_{1}\times\mathfrak{C}_{2}
and the fact that
x\in\mathfrak{C}_{1}(\{x\}),y\in\mathfrak{C}_{2}(\{y\})
for all
(x,y)\in r
. Let now
r\subseteq r^{\prime}
and suppose
(x,y)\in(\mathfrak{C}_{1}\times\mathfrak{C}_{2})(r)
. Then there exist
x^{\prime},y^{\prime}
such that
(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})\in r
and
x\in\mathfrak{C}_{1}(\{x^{\prime}\}),y\in\mathfrak{C}_{2}(\{y^{\prime}\})
. Then by the assumption
(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})\in r^{\prime}
and by definition
(x,y)\in(\mathfrak{C}_{1}\times\mathfrak{C}_{2})(r^{\prime})
.
Finally, if
(x,y)\in(\mathfrak{C}_{1}\times\mathfrak{C}_{2})^{2}(r)
, then there exist
x^{\prime},y^{\prime}
such that
x\in\mathfrak{C}_{1}(\{x^{\prime}\})
,
y\in\mathfrak{C}_{2}(\{y^{\prime}\})
and
(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})\in(\mathfrak{C}_{1}\times\mathfrak{C}_{2})(r)
, that is, there exist
x^{\prime\prime},y^{\prime\prime}
such that
x^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{C}_{1}(\{x^{\prime\prime}\})
,
y^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{C}_{2}(\{y^{\prime\prime}\})
and
(x^{\prime\prime},y^{\prime\prime})\in r
.
Since
x^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{C}_{1}(\{x^{\prime\prime}\})
, we have
\mathfrak{C}_{1}(\{x^{\prime}\})\subseteq\mathfrak{C}_{1}(\mathfrak{C}_{1}(\{x^{\prime\prime}\}))=\mathfrak{C}_{1}(\{x^{\prime\prime}\})
, whence
x\in\mathfrak{C}_{1}(\{x^{\prime\prime}\})
. Similarly,
y\in\mathfrak{C}_{2}(\{y^{\prime\prime}\})
. We deduce then that
(x,y)\in(\mathfrak{C}_{1}\times\mathfrak{C}_{2})(r)
.

If
r=\emptyset
, then Property 1. is trivial; Property 2. follows from the fact that
\mathfrak{C}_{1}(\emptyset)\subseteq s
, for all
s\in S_{\mathfrak{C}_{1}}
and
\mathfrak{C}_{2}(\emptyset)\subseteq t
, for all
t\in S_{\mathfrak{C}_{2}}
. Finally, Property 3. follows from the fact that if
(P,Q)\in(\mathfrak{C}_{1}\times\mathfrak{C}_{2})^{2}(\emptyset)
, then
P\in\mathfrak{C}_{1}(\emptyset),Q\in\mathfrak{C}_{2}(\emptyset)
, hence
(P,Q)\in(\mathfrak{C}_{1}\times\mathfrak{C}_{2})(\emptyset)
.

Observe that, by definition, if
r\subseteq L_{1}\times L_{2}
is non-empty, then

(\mathfrak{C}_{1}\times\mathfrak{C}_{2})(r)=\bigcup_{(x,y)\in r}\mathfrak{C}_{1}(\{x\})\times\mathfrak{C}_{2}(\{y\}),

hence
\mathfrak{C}_{1}\times\mathfrak{C}_{2}
is stable by union.

We call a relation
r\subseteq L_{1}\times L_{2}

(
\mathfrak{C}_{1}\times\mathfrak{C}_{2}
)-closed
if
(\mathfrak{C}_{1}\times\mathfrak{C}_{2})(r)=r
.

Proposition 57
.

If
\mathfrak{C}_{1},\mathfrak{C}_{2}
are closure operators over
L_{1},L_{2}
, then for any
(\mathfrak{C}_{1}\times\mathfrak{C}_{2})
-closed relation
r\subseteq L_{1}\times L_{2}
,

r=\bigcup_{(x,y)\in r}\mathfrak{C}_{1}(\{x\})\times\mathfrak{C}_{2}(\{y\})

Proof 5.2
.

Let
r
be
(\mathfrak{C}_{1}\times\mathfrak{C}_{2})
-closed and let
r^{*}=\bigcup_{(x,y)\in r}\mathfrak{C}_{1}(\{x\})\times\mathfrak{C}_{2}(\{r\})
.
From Definition
5.1.1
it follows that if
(x,y)\in r
,
x^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{C}_{1}(\{x\})
and
y^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{C}_{2}(\{y\})
, then
(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})\in r
. This shows that
r^{*}\subseteq r
.
For the converse direction, if
(x,y)\in r
, as
x\in\mathfrak{C}_{1}(\{x\})
and
y\in\mathfrak{C}_{2}(\{y\})
, then
(x,y)\in r^{*}
.

When
\mathfrak{C}_{1},\mathfrak{C}_{2}
satisfy
\mathsf{SU}
, then the associated topologies are generated by the closure of singletons. Then Proposition
57
and the fact that
\mathfrak{C}_{1}\times\mathfrak{C}_{2}
always satisfies
\mathsf{SU}
assures that, in this case, the
(\mathfrak{C}_{1}\times\mathfrak{C}_{2})
-closed relations (plus the empty relation) correspond to the product topology.

We recall that the product topology has the following universal property: it is the coarsest topology for which projections are continuous.
We formulate and prove this property in our framework:

Proposition 58
.

Let
L_{1},L_{2}\subseteq\Lambda
,
\mathfrak{C}_{1},\mathfrak{C}_{2}
be closure operators over
L_{1}
and
L_{2}
, respectively, and let
\pi_{i}:L_{1}\times L_{2}\to L_{i}
, for
i=1,2
be the projection maps
\pi_{i}((P_{1},P_{2}))=P_{i}
. Then for all
i=1,2
and
s\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{C}_{i}}
,
\pi_{i}^{-1}(s)
is
(\mathfrak{C}_{1}\times\mathfrak{C}_{2})
-closed.
Moreover, for all closure operators
\mathfrak{D}
over
L_{1}\times L_{2}
, if for all
i=1,2
and
s\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{C}_{i}}
,
\pi_{i}^{-1}(s)
is
\mathfrak{D}
-closed, then
\mathfrak{D}\leq\mathfrak{C}_{1}\times\mathfrak{C}_{2}
.

Proof 5.3
.

For all
s\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{C}_{1}}
, it is clear that
\pi_{1}^{-1}(s)=s\times L_{2}
is
(\mathfrak{C}_{1}\times\mathfrak{C}_{2})
-closed, and similarly for
s\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{C}_{2}}
.
Suppose now
\mathfrak{D}
is a closure operator
L_{1}\times L_{2}
satisfying
\mathsf{SU}
and such that, for all
i=1,2
and
s\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{C}_{i}}
,
\pi_{i}^{-1}(s)
is
\mathfrak{D}
-closed.
We must prove that for all
r\subseteq L_{1}\times L_{2}
, if
r
is
(\mathfrak{C}_{1}\times\mathfrak{C}_{2})
-closed, then
r
is
\mathfrak{D}
-closed. If
r\neq\emptyset
is
(\mathfrak{C}_{1}\times\mathfrak{C}_{2})
-closed, then by Proposition
57
,

r=\bigcup_{(P,Q)\in r}\mathfrak{C}_{1}(\{P\})\times\mathfrak{C}_{2}(\{Q\})
. Observe that, for all
P\in L_{1},Q\in L_{2}
,

\mathfrak{C}_{1}(\{P\})\times\mathfrak{C}_{2}(\{Q\})=(\mathfrak{C}_{1}(\{P\})\times L_{2})\cap(L_{1}\times\mathfrak{C}_{2}(\{Q\}))=\pi_{1}^{-1}(\mathfrak{C}_{1}(\{P\}))\cap\pi_{2}^{-1}(\mathfrak{C}_{2}(\{Q\}))
.
By the assumption we deduce then that
\mathfrak{C}_{1}(\{P\})\times\mathfrak{C}_{2}(\{Q\})
is
\mathfrak{D}
-closed, and since
\mathfrak{D}
is stable by union,

r=\bigcup_{(P,Q)\in r}\mathfrak{C}_{1}(\{P\})\times\mathfrak{C}_{2}(\{Q\})
is
\mathfrak{D}
-closed.
If
r=\emptyset
is
(\mathfrak{C}_{1}\times\mathfrak{C}_{2})
-closed, then it must be either
\mathfrak{C}_{1}(\emptyset)=\emptyset
or
\mathfrak{C}_{2}(\emptyset)=\emptyset
. Suppose
\mathfrak{C}_{1}(\emptyset)=\emptyset
, then
\pi_{1}^{-1}(\emptyset)=\emptyset
is
\mathfrak{D}
-closed. We can argue similarly if
\mathfrak{C}_{2}(\emptyset)=\emptyset
.

5.1.2.
\mathfrak{C}
-closed logical relations

In this subsection we consider given an adequate
\mathsf{F}
-closure operator
\mathfrak{C}
over
L\subseteq\Lambda
satisfying
\mathsf{SU}
and
\mathsf{SA}
(as discussed in Section
3
all concrete closure operators considered are of this form).
We introduce the notion of
\mathfrak{C}
-closed relation:

{defi}

[
\mathfrak{C}
-closed logical relation]
A
\mathfrak{C}
-closed logical relation
is a binary relation
r\subseteq s\times t
, for some
s,t\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{C}}
, such that
r=(\mathfrak{C}\times\mathfrak{C})(r)
.
We let
\mathcal{R}_{\mathfrak{C}}(s,t)
indicate the set of
\mathfrak{C}
-closed relations over
s,t\in S
and
\mathcal{R}_{\mathfrak{C}}:=\mathcal{R}_{\mathfrak{C}}(L,L)=\bigcup_{s,t\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{C}}}\mathcal{R}_{\mathfrak{C}}(s,t)
.

By the results of the previous subsections
(L\times L,\mathcal{R}_{\mathfrak{C}}\cup\{\emptyset\})
is the topological space given by the product topology over
(L,\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{C}}\cup\{\emptyset\})
.

The following is easily verified from Definition
5.1.1
.

Lemma 59
.

Let
s,t\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{C}}
. If
r\subseteq s\times t
is non-empty, then
r\in\mathcal{R}_{\mathfrak{C}}(s,t)
iff for all
(P,Q)\in r
and
P^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{C}(\{P\})
,
Q^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{C}(\{Q\})
,
(P^{\prime},Q^{\prime})\in r
.

Proposition 60
.

If
s,s^{\prime},t,t^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{C}}
,
r\subseteq s\times t
and
r^{\prime}\subseteq s^{\prime}\times t^{\prime}
are
\mathfrak{C}
-closed relations, then the relation
r\to r^{\prime}\subseteq(s\to_{L}s^{\prime})\times(t\to_{L}t^{\prime})
defined by
P\ (r\to r^{\prime})\ Q
if for all
M\in s,N\in t
, if
M\ r\ N
then
(PM)\ r^{\prime}\ (QN)
, is a
\mathfrak{C}
-closed relation.

Proof 5.4
.

We must show that
r^{\prime\prime}=r\to r^{\prime}
is
\mathfrak{C}
-closed.
Suppose
(F,G)\in r^{\prime\prime}
and let
F^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{C}(\{F\}),G^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{C}(\{G\})
. If
r^{\prime\prime}
is non-empty, by Lemma
59
we must show that
(F^{\prime},G^{\prime})\in r^{\prime\prime}
.

Suppose
r\neq\emptyset
, so that there exist
P\in s,Q\in t
such that
(P,Q)\in r
; since
\mathfrak{C}
satisfies
\mathsf{SA}
, if
F^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{C}(\{F\})
and
FP\in L
, then
F^{\prime}P\in\mathfrak{C}(\{FP\})
. Similarly, if
G^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{C}(\{G\})
and
GQ\in L
, then
G^{\prime}Q\in\mathfrak{C}(\{GQ\})
.
Hence, since
(FP,GQ)\in r^{\prime}
, it must be
FP,GQ\in L
. Moreover, as

r^{\prime}
is
\mathfrak{C}
-closed, we deduce that
(F^{\prime}P,G^{\prime}Q)\in r^{\prime}
, so we conclude that
(F^{\prime},G^{\prime})\in r^{\prime\prime}
.
Suppose
r=\emptyset
: then
r^{\prime\prime}=(s\to_{L}s^{\prime})\times(t\to_{L}t^{\prime})
so
(F^{\prime},G^{\prime})\in r^{\prime\prime}
.

Let now
r^{\prime\prime}=\emptyset
. We claim that
r^{\prime}=\emptyset
: if
(P,Q)\in r^{\prime}
, then if
x\notin\mathtt{FV}(P)\cup\mathtt{FV}(Q)
,
\lambda x.P\in s\to_{L}s^{\prime}
and
\lambda x.Q\in t\to_{L}t^{\prime}
by condition
\mathsf{K}
and
(\lambda x.P,\lambda x.Q)\in r^{\prime\prime}
, against the assumption. Since
r^{\prime}
is
\mathfrak{C}
-closed, we deduce
\mathfrak{C}(\emptyset)=\emptyset
, whence
r^{\prime\prime}
is
\mathfrak{C}
-closed.

Proposition 61
.

Let
I\neq\emptyset
and let, for all
i\in I
,
s_{i},t_{i}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{C}}
and
r_{i}\in\mathcal{R}_{\mathfrak{C}}(s_{i},t_{i})
. Then
\bigcap_{i\in I}r_{i}\in\mathcal{R}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\bigcap_{i\in I}s_{i},\bigcap_{i\in I}t_{i})
.

Proof 5.5
.

Let
r=\bigcap_{i\in I}r_{i}
,
s=\bigcap_{i\in I}s_{i},t=\bigcap_{i\in I}t_{i}
.
We claim that
(\mathfrak{C}\times\mathfrak{C})(r)=r
: suppose
P\in s,Q\in t
,
(P,Q)\in r
and let
P^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{C}(\{P\}),Q^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{C}(\{Q\})
; we must show that
(P^{\prime},Q^{\prime})\in r
. For any
i\in I
, we have then
(P,Q)\in r_{i}
and, since
r_{i}
is a
\mathfrak{C}
-closed relation,
(P^{\prime},Q^{\prime})\in r_{i}
. We conclude thus
(P^{\prime},Q^{\prime})\in r
.

The characterizations of the closure operators considered in Section
3
(Propositions
31
and
38
) induce the following characterization of the associated closed relations:

Proposition 62
.

•

r\subseteq\Lambda\times\Lambda
is a
(\_)^{\beta}
-closed logical relation iff for all
P,Q\in\Lambda
, if
(P,Q)\in r
,
P\simeq_{\beta}P^{\prime}
and
Q\simeq_{\beta}Q^{\prime}
, then
(P,Q)\in r
;

•

r\subseteq\Lambda\times\Lambda
is a
(\_)^{\beta\eta}
-closed logical relation iff for all
P,Q\in\Lambda
, if
(P,Q)\in r
,
P\simeq_{\beta\eta}P^{\prime}
and
Q\simeq_{\beta\eta}Q^{\prime}
, then
(P,Q)\in r
;

•

r\subseteq\Lambda\times\Lambda
is a
(\_)^{\beta sat}
-closed logical relation iff for all
P,Q\in\Lambda
, if
(P,Q)\in r
and if
P=M[P^{\prime}/x]P_{1}\dots P_{n}
(resp.
Q=N[Q^{\prime}/y]Q_{1}\dots Q_{m}
), then
((\lambda x.M)PP_{1}\dots P_{n},Q)\in r
(resp.
(P,(\lambda y.N)QQ_{1}\dots Q_{m})\in r
);

•

r\subseteq\mathcal{SN}\times\mathcal{SN}
is a
{\mathsf{CR}}
-closed logical relation iff the following hold:

–

if
(P,Q)\in r
,
P\to^{*}_{\beta}P^{\prime},Q\to^{*}_{\beta}Q^{\prime}
, then
(P^{\prime},Q),(P,Q^{\prime})\in r
;

–

if
P\in\mathcal{N}
(resp.
Q\in\mathcal{N}
) and for all
P^{\prime}
(resp.
Q^{\prime}
) such that
P\to_{\beta}P^{\prime}
(resp.
Q\to_{\beta}Q^{\prime}
),
(P^{\prime},Q)\in r
(resp.
(P,Q^{\prime})\in r
), then
(P,Q)\in r
.

•

r\subseteq\mathcal{SN}\times\mathcal{SN}
is a
{\mathsf{CR}^{\prime}}
-closed logical relation iff the following hold:

–

if
(P,Q)\in r
and
P\to^{*}_{\beta}P^{\prime},Q\to^{*}_{\beta}Q^{\prime}
, then
(P^{\prime},Q),(P,Q^{\prime})\in r
;

–

if
P\in\mathcal{N}^{*}
(resp.
Q\in\mathcal{N}^{*}
) and for all
P^{\prime}
(resp.
Q^{\prime}
) such that
P\to_{\beta}P^{\prime}
(resp.
Q\to_{\beta}Q^{\prime}
),
(P^{\prime},Q)\in r
(resp.
(P,Q^{\prime})\in r
), then
(P,Q)\in r
.

•

r\subseteq\mathcal{SN}\times\mathcal{SN}
is a
{\mathsf{CR}^{\prime\prime}}
-closed logical relation iff the following hold:

–

if
(P,Q)\in r
and
P\to^{*}_{\beta}P^{\prime},Q\to^{*}_{\beta}Q^{\prime}
, then
(P^{\prime},Q),(P,Q^{\prime})\in r
;

–

given
P,Q,M\in\mathcal{SN}
and
F_{1}\dots F_{n}\in\mathcal{N}^{*}
, if

P=M[F_{1}/x_{1},\dots,F_{n}/x_{n}]
(resp.
Q=M[F_{1}/x_{1},\dots,F_{n}/x_{n}]
) and for all
G_{1},\dots,G_{n}
such that

F_{i}\to_{\beta}G_{i}
,

(M[G_{1}/x_{1},\dots,G_{n}/x_{n}],Q)\in r
(resp.
(P,M[G_{1}/x_{1},\dots,G_{n}/x_{n})\in r
), then
(P,Q)\in r
.

5.1.3.
Soundness for
\mathfrak{C}
-closed logical relations

We now show that for all System
\mathsf{F}
type judgement
\Gamma\vdash M:\sigma
, the interpretation of
M
in the semantics generated by
\mathfrak{C}
preserves all
\mathfrak{C}
-closed logical relations.

{defi}

[
\mathfrak{C}
-closed relation assignment]
For any type
\sigma\in\mathtt{T}
and
\mathcal{X}=\{X_{1},\dots,X_{n}\}\subseteq\mathtt{TypeVar}
such that
\mathtt{FV}(\sigma)\subseteq\mathcal{X}
and
\mathcal{X}\cap\mathtt{BV}(\sigma)=\emptyset
, we define a map

\langle\sigma\rangle^{\mathfrak{C},\mathcal{X}}:\mathcal{R}_{\mathfrak{C}}^{n}\to\mathcal{R}_{\mathfrak{C}}
as follows:

\begin{split}\langle{X_{i}}\rangle^{\mathfrak{C},\mathcal{X}}(r_{1},\dots,r_{n})&=r_{i}\\
\langle{\sigma\to\tau}\rangle^{\mathfrak{C},\mathcal{X}}(r_{1},\dots,r_{n})&=\langle{\sigma}\rangle^{\mathfrak{C},\mathcal{X}}(r_{1},\dots,r_{n})\to\langle{\tau}\rangle^{\mathfrak{C},\mathcal{X}}(r_{1},\dots,r_{n})\\
\langle{\forall X\sigma}\rangle^{\mathfrak{C},\mathcal{X}}(r_{1},\dots,r_{n})&=\bigcap_{s,t\in S}\left(\bigcap_{r\in\mathcal{R}_{S}(s,t)}\langle{\sigma}\rangle^{\mathfrak{C},\{X\}\cup\mathcal{X}}(r,r_{1},\dots,r_{n})\right)\end{split}

(9)

We let
\langle\sigma\rangle^{\mathfrak{C}}:=\langle\sigma\rangle^{\mathfrak{C},\mathtt{FV}(\sigma)}:\mathcal{R}_{\mathfrak{C}}^{card(\mathtt{FV}(\sigma))}\to\mathcal{R}_{\mathfrak{C}}
.

Let
\mathcal{M}_{1},\mathcal{M}_{2}:\mathtt{TypeVar}\to S
be two
\mathfrak{C}
-interpretations. A
\mathfrak{C}
-closed relation assignment
R
over
\mathcal{M}_{1}
and
\mathcal{M}_{2}
is a map associating, with any variable
X
, a
\mathfrak{C}
-closed relation
R(X)\in\mathcal{R}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\mathcal{M}_{1}(X),\mathcal{M}_{2}(X))
.

Given a
\mathfrak{C}
-closed relation assignment
R
over
\mathfrak{C}
-interpretations
\mathcal{M}_{1}
and
\mathcal{M}_{2}
, for any type
\sigma\in\mathtt{T}
, with
\mathtt{FV}(\sigma)\subseteq\{X_{1},\dots,X_{n}\}
, we
let
\langle\sigma\rangle^{\mathfrak{C}}_{R}:=\langle\sigma\rangle^{\mathfrak{C}}(R(X_{1}),\dots,R(X_{n}))
.

The following is easily verified by induction on types:

Lemma 63
.

Given a
\mathfrak{C}
-closed relation assignment
R
over
\mathfrak{C}
-interpretations
\mathcal{M}_{1}
and
\mathcal{M}_{2}
, for any type
\sigma\in\mathtt{T}
,
\langle\sigma\rangle^{\mathfrak{C}}_{R}\in\mathcal{R}_{\mathfrak{C}}(|\sigma|^{\mathfrak{C}}_{\mathcal{M}_{1}},|\sigma|^{\mathfrak{C}}_{\mathcal{M}_{2}})
.

Given a
\mathfrak{C}
-closed relation assignment
R
over
\mathcal{M}_{1},\mathcal{M}_{2}:\mathtt{TypeVar}\to S
, a type
\sigma
and terms
P\in|\sigma|^{\mathfrak{C}}_{\mathcal{M}_{1}},Q\in|\sigma|^{\mathfrak{C}}_{\mathcal{M}_{2}}
, we will often indicate
(P,Q)\in\langle\sigma\rangle^{\mathfrak{C}}_{R}
by
P\ \langle\sigma\rangle^{\mathfrak{C}}_{R}\ Q
.

The following lemma and proposition extend Lemma
29
and Proposition
30
to the case of
\mathfrak{C}
-closed relations.

Lemma 64
.

Let
I\neq\emptyset
,
r,t\subseteq L\times L
and, for all
i\in I
,
s_{i}\subseteq\Lambda\times\Lambda
. Then

(i)

r\to\bigcap_{i\in I}s_{i}=\bigcap_{i\in I}(r\to s_{i})
;

(ii)

(\bigcap_{i\in I}s_{i}\to r)\to t\subseteq\bigcap_{i\in I}((s_{i}\to r)\to t)
.

Proof 5.6
.

The argument is similar to that of Lemma
29
.

Proposition 65
.

Let
\mathcal{M}_{1},\mathcal{M}_{2}
be
\mathfrak{C}
-interpretations and
R
a
\mathfrak{C}
-closed relation assignment over them.

(i)

If
\sigma\in\forall^{+}
, then
\langle\sigma\rangle^{\mathfrak{C}}_{R}\subseteq\langle\sigma^{+}\rangle^{\mathfrak{C}}_{R}
;

(ii)

if
\sigma\in\forall^{-}
, then
\langle\sigma^{-}\rangle^{\mathfrak{C}}_{R}\subseteq\langle\sigma\rangle^{\mathfrak{C}}_{R}
.

Proof 5.7
.

The argument is similar to that of Proposition
30
.

{defi}

[
\mathfrak{C}
-invariance]
Let
\sigma\in\mathtt{T}
and
M\in\|\sigma\|^{\mathfrak{C}}
.
M
is
\mathfrak{C}
-invariant at
\sigma
if, for all
\mathfrak{C}
-interpretations
\mathcal{M}_{1},\mathcal{M}_{2}
and
\mathfrak{C}
-closed logical relation assignment
R
over
\mathcal{M}_{1},\mathcal{M}_{2}
,
M\ \langle\sigma\rangle^{\mathfrak{C}}_{R}\ M
.

The following lemma establishes a relational version of condition
\mathsf{K}
(Subsection
3.1.3
).

Lemma 66
.

Let
s,s^{\prime},t,t^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{C}}
,
r\in\mathcal{R}_{\mathfrak{C}}(s,s^{\prime})
and
r^{\prime}\in\mathcal{R}_{\mathfrak{C}}(t,t^{\prime})
. Let
P,Q\in L
be such that, for all
F\in s,G\in s^{\prime}
,
P[F/x]\in t
and
Q[G/y]\in t^{\prime}
. Then, if for all
F\in s,G\in s^{\prime}
such that
(F,G)\in r
,
(P[F/x],Q[G/y])\in r^{\prime}
, then
(\lambda x.P,\lambda y.Q)\in r\to r^{\prime}
.

Proof 5.8
.

Since
\mathfrak{C}
is adequate, from the hypotheses it follows that
\lambda x.P\in s\to_{L}t
and
\lambda y.Q\in s^{\prime}\to_{L}t^{\prime}
.
Suppose that for all
F\in s,G\in s^{\prime}
such that
(F,G)\in r
,
(P[F/x],Q[G/y])\in r
.
Let
F\in s
,
s_{0}=\{F\}
and
t_{0}=\{P[F^{\prime}/x]\mid F^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{C}(s_{0})\}
; observe that, since
\mathfrak{C}(s_{0})\subseteq s
and for all
F\in s
,
P[F/x]\in t
, it follows that
\mathfrak{C}(t_{0})\subseteq t
. Now, by condition
\mathsf{K}
,
\lambda x.P\in\mathfrak{C}(s_{0})\to\mathfrak{C}(t_{0})
, whence
(\lambda x.P)F\in\mathfrak{C}(t_{0})\subseteq t
. Let
G\in s
,
s^{\prime}_{0}=\{G\}
and
t^{\prime}_{0}=\{Q[G^{\prime}/y]\mid G^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{C}(s^{\prime}_{0})\}
; by a similar argument we deduce that
(\lambda y.Q)G\in\mathfrak{C}(t^{\prime}_{0})\subseteq t^{\prime}
.
By
\mathsf{SU}
there exist then
F^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{C}(s_{0}),G^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{C}(s^{\prime}_{0})
such that
(\lambda x.P)F\in\mathfrak{C}(\{P[F^{\prime}/x]\})
and
(\lambda y.Q)G\in\mathfrak{C}(\{Q[G^{\prime}/y]\})
.

Suppose now
r\neq\emptyset
and let
(F,G)\in r
. Since
r
is a
\mathfrak{C}
-closed relation,
(F^{\prime},G^{\prime})\in r
, whence, by the hypothesis,
(P[F^{\prime}/x],Q[G^{\prime}/y])\in r^{\prime}
and, since
r^{\prime}
is a
\mathfrak{C}
-closed relation, we deduce
((\lambda x.P)F,(\lambda y.Q)G)\in r^{\prime}
. We can conclude then
(\lambda x.P,\lambda y.Q)\in r\to r^{\prime}
.
If
r=\emptyset
, then
r\to r^{\prime}=(s\to_{L}t)\times(s^{\prime}\to_{L}t^{\prime})
, hence
(\lambda x.P,\lambda y.Q)\in r\to r^{\prime}
.

Lemma 67
.

For any
\sigma,\tau\in\mathtt{T}
, with
\mathtt{FV}(\sigma)=\{X,X_{1},\dots,X_{n}\}
and

\mathtt{FV}(\tau)=\{X_{1},\dots,X_{n}\}
, and for all
r_{1},\dots,r_{n}\in\mathcal{R}_{\mathfrak{C}}
,

\langle\sigma\rangle^{\mathfrak{C}}(\langle\tau\rangle^{\mathfrak{C}}(\vec{r}),\vec{r})=\langle\sigma[\tau/X]\rangle^{\mathfrak{C}}(\vec{r})
.

Proof 5.9
.

The proof is similar to that of Lemma
25
.

Theorem 68
.

Let
\Gamma\vdash M:\sigma
, where
\Gamma=\{x_{1}:\sigma_{1},\dots,x_{n}:\sigma_{n}\}
and

\mathtt{FV}(\Gamma)\cup\mathtt{FV}(\sigma)\subseteq\{X_{1},\dots,X_{p}\}
. Then for any two
\mathfrak{C}
-interpretations
\mathcal{M}_{1},\mathcal{M}_{2}
and
\mathfrak{C}
-closed relation assignment
R
over
\mathcal{M}_{1},\mathcal{M}_{2}
, for any
F_{1}\in|\sigma_{1}|^{\mathfrak{C}}_{\mathcal{M}_{1}},G_{1}\in|\sigma_{1}|^{\mathfrak{C}}_{\mathcal{M}_{2}},\dots,F_{n}\in|\sigma_{n}|^{\mathfrak{C}}_{\mathcal{M}_{1}},G_{n}\in|\sigma_{n}|^{\mathfrak{C}}_{\mathcal{M}_{2}}
,
if
F_{i}\ \langle\sigma_{i}\rangle^{\mathfrak{C}}_{R}\ G_{i}
for
i=1,\dots,n
, then

M[F_{1}/x_{1},\dots,F_{n}/x_{n}]\ \langle\sigma\rangle^{\mathfrak{C}}_{R}\ M[G_{1}/x_{1},\dots,G_{n}/x_{n}]
.

Proof 5.10
.

Induction on a typing derivation of
\Gamma\vdash M:\sigma
.

•

If the derivation is
\Gamma,x:\sigma\vdash x:\sigma
, then
x[F/x]=F\ \langle\sigma\rangle^{\mathfrak{C}}_{R}\ G=x[G/x]
.

•

If the derivation ends by

\Gamma,x:\sigma\vdash M:\tau

\Gamma\vdash\lambda x.M:\sigma\to\tau

then we can assume that
x
does not occur free in
F_{1},G_{1},\dots,F_{n},G_{n}
and is different from
x_{1},\dots,x_{n}
. By the induction hypothesis, for all
F\in|\sigma|_{\mathcal{M}_{1}}^{\mathfrak{C}},G\in|\sigma|_{\mathcal{M}_{2}}^{\mathfrak{C}}
,
M[F_{1}/x_{1},\dots,F_{n}/x_{n},F/x]\ \langle\tau\rangle^{\mathfrak{C}}_{R}\ M[G_{1}/x_{1},\dots,G_{n}/x_{n},G/x]
and by the assumptions made,
M[F_{1}/x_{1},\dots,F_{n}/x_{n},F/x]=M[F_{1}/x_{1},\dots,F_{n}/x_{n}][F/x]
and
M[G_{1}/x_{1},\dots,G_{n}/x_{n},G/x]=M[G_{1}/x_{1},\dots,G_{n}/x_{n}][G/x]
. Then we can conclude
(\lambda x.M)[F_{1}/x_{1},\dots,F_{n}/x_{n}]\ \langle\sigma\to\tau\rangle^{\mathfrak{C}}_{R}\ (\lambda x.M)[G_{1}/x_{1},\dots,G_{n}/x_{n}]
by Lemma
66
.

•

If the derivation ends by

\Gamma\vdash M_{1}:\tau\to\sigma

\Gamma\vdash M_{2}:\tau

\Gamma\vdash M_{1}M_{2}:\sigma

, then by the induction hypothesis
M_{1}[F_{1}/x_{1},\dots,F_{n}/x_{n}]\ \langle\tau\to\sigma\rangle^{\mathfrak{C}}_{R}\ M_{1}[G_{1}/x_{1},\dots,G_{n}/x_{n}]
and

M_{2}[F_{1}/x_{1},\dots,F_{n}/x_{n}]\ \langle\tau\rangle^{\mathfrak{C}}_{R}\ M_{2}[G_{1}/x_{1},\dots,G_{n}/x_{n}]
. We conclude that

M_{1}M_{2}[F_{1}/x_{1},\dots,F_{n}/x_{n}]\ \langle\sigma\rangle^{\mathfrak{C}}_{R}\ M_{1}M_{2}[G_{1}/x_{1},\dots,G_{n}/x_{n}]
.

•

If the derivation ends by

\Gamma\vdash M:\sigma

\Gamma\vdash M:\forall X\sigma

, where
X\notin\mathtt{FV}(\Gamma)
, then for all
s,t\in S
,
r\in\mathcal{R}^{\mathfrak{C}}(s,t)
and
i\leq n
,
\langle\sigma_{i}\rangle^{\mathfrak{C}}(r,R(X_{1}),\dots,R(X_{p}))=\langle\sigma_{i}\rangle^{\mathfrak{C}}_{R}
, whence by the induction hypothesis we deduce

M[F_{1}/x_{1},\dots,F_{n}/x_{n}]\ \langle\sigma\rangle^{\mathfrak{C}}(r,R(X_{1}),\dots,R(X_{p}))\ M[G_{1}/x_{1},\dots,G_{n}/x_{n}]
, for all
s,t\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{C}}
and
r\in\mathcal{R}_{\mathfrak{C}}(s,t)
.

We finally conclude then
M[F_{1}/x_{1},\dots,F_{n}/x_{n}]\ \langle\forall X\sigma\rangle^{\mathfrak{C}}_{R}\ M[G_{1}/x_{1},\dots,G_{n}/x_{n}]
.

•

If the derivation ends by

\Gamma\vdash M:\forall X\sigma

\Gamma\vdash M:\sigma[\tau/x]

, then by the induction hypothesis, for all
s,t\in S
and
r\in\mathcal{R}^{\mathfrak{C}}(s,t)
,
M[F_{1}/x_{1},\dots,F_{n}/x_{n}]\ \langle\sigma\rangle^{\mathfrak{C}}(r,R(X_{1}),\dots,R(X_{p}))\ M[G_{1}/x_{1},\dots,G_{n}/x_{n}]
, hence in particular

M[F_{1}/x_{1},\dots,F_{n}/x_{n}]\ \langle\sigma\rangle^{\mathfrak{C}}(\langle\tau\rangle^{\mathfrak{C}}_{R},R(X_{1}),\dots,R(X_{p}))\ M[G_{1}/x_{1},\dots,G_{n}/x_{n}].

The claim then follows from Lemma
67
.

5.2.
The
\mathfrak{C}
-invariance theorem

We prove that
\mathfrak{C}
-interpretable terms are
\mathfrak{C}
-invariant, when
\mathfrak{C}
satisfies
\mathsf{SU}
,
\mathsf{SA}
and
\mathsf{SS}
. Such conditions hold for the semantics
(\_)^{\beta},(\_)^{\beta\eta},(\_)^{\beta sat}
. As a corollary, we obtain that terms intepretable in the semantics generated by either
\mathsf{CR}^{\prime}
or
\mathsf{CR}^{\prime\prime}
are invariant with respect to
\beta
and
\beta\eta
-stable logical relations.

The proof of Theorem
71
is obtained by adapting the “term model” of Section
4
to the case of
\mathfrak{C}
-closed logical relations.
Theorem
71
generalizes a result in
[
Pis17
]
, showing that the implication between realizability and Reynolds’ parametricity in the
\beta\eta
-stable semantics holds for simple types (see Subsection
5.3
).

The infinite context
\Gamma^{\infty}
(Definition
4.1
) will be here used to define contextual notions of
\mathfrak{C}
-interpretation and
\mathfrak{C}
-closed relation assignment. A special interpretation
\mathcal{M}_{\mathtt{p}}
will be defined such that, for any type
\sigma
and
\lambda
-term
P
, if
P\in|\sigma|_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathtt{p}}}^{\mathfrak{C}}
, then for any two
\mathfrak{C}
-interpretations and
\mathfrak{C}
-closed relation assignment over them,
P
is contextually related to
P
relative to
\sigma
.

In addition to
\mathsf{SU}
and
\mathsf{SA}
, which are presupposed by our treatment of
\mathfrak{C}
-closed logical relations, we use stability by substitution
(\mathsf{SS})
to show that the contextual typability relations and relation assignments form, respectively,
\mathfrak{C}
-closed sets and
\mathfrak{C}
-closed relations.

As in the previous subsection, we will suppose
\mathfrak{C}
to be an adequate
\mathsf{F}
-closure operator over
L\subseteq\Lambda
satisfying
\mathsf{SU}
and
\mathsf{SA}
.

{defi}

•

Given
\mathcal{M}:\mathtt{TypeVar}\to\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{C}}
,
P\in\Lambda
and
\tau\in\mathtt{T}
, we let the statement
P\in|\Gamma^{\infty}\vdash\tau|_{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathfrak{C}}
hold when, by letting
FV(P)=\{x_{i_{1}},\dots,x_{i_{n}}\}
, for every
Q_{1}\in|\tau_{i_{1}}|^{\mathfrak{C}}_{\mathcal{M}},\dots,Q_{n}\in|\tau_{i_{n}}|^{\mathfrak{C}}_{\mathcal{M}}
,

P[Q_{1}/x_{i_{1}},\dots,Q_{n}/x_{i_{n}}]\in|\tau|_{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathfrak{C}}
;

•

given
\mathcal{M}_{1},\mathcal{M}_{2}:\mathtt{TypeVar}\to\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{C}}
and
R
a
\mathfrak{C}
-closed relation assignment over
\mathcal{M}_{1},\mathcal{M}_{2}
,
P,Q\in\Lambda
and
\tau\in\mathtt{T}
, we let the statement
P\ \langle\Gamma^{\infty}\vdash\tau\rangle^{\mathfrak{C}}_{R}\ Q
hold when
P\in|\Gamma^{\infty}\vdash\tau|_{\mathcal{M}_{1}}^{\mathfrak{C}}
,
Q\in|\Gamma^{\infty}\vdash\tau|_{\mathcal{M}_{2}}^{\mathfrak{C}}
and, by letting
FV(P)\cup FV(Q)=\{x_{i_{1}},\dots,x_{i_{n}}\}
,
for every
F_{j}\in|\tau_{i_{j}}|_{\mathcal{M}_{1}}^{\mathfrak{C}},G_{j}\in|\tau_{i_{j}}|_{\mathcal{M}_{2}}^{\mathfrak{C}}
such that

F_{j}\ \langle\tau_{i_{j}}\rangle^{\mathfrak{C}}_{R}\ G_{j}
for
1\leq j\leq n
, we have

P[F_{1}/x_{i_{1}},\dots,F_{n}/x_{i_{n}}]\ \langle\tau\rangle^{\mathfrak{C}}_{R}\ Q[G_{1}/x_{i_{1}},\dots,G_{n}/x_{i_{n}}]

(10)

The following lemmas assure that, if
\mathfrak{C}
satisfies
\mathsf{SS}
, Definition
5.2
introduces
\mathfrak{C}
-closed sets and
\mathfrak{C}
-closed relations, respectively.

Lemma 69
.

If
\mathfrak{C}
satisfies
\mathsf{SS}
, then for all
s,t\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{C}}
,
r\in\mathcal{R}_{\mathfrak{C}}(s,t)
,
for all
P,P^{\prime},Q,Q^{\prime},F,G\in L
,
P^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{C}(\{P\}),Q^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{C}(\{Q\})
, if
(P[F_{1}/x_{1},\dots,F_{n}/x_{n}],Q[G_{1}/y_{1},\dots,G_{n}/y_{n}])\in r
, then

(P^{\prime}[F_{1}/x_{1},\dots,F_{n}/x_{n}],Q^{\prime}[G_{1}/y_{1},\dots,G_{n}/y_{n}])\in r
;

Proof 5.11
.

The claim follows from
P^{\prime}[F_{1}/x_{1},\dots,F_{n}/x_{n}]\in\mathfrak{C}(\{P[F_{1}/x_{1},\dots,F_{n}/x_{n}]\})
and

Q^{\prime}[G_{1}/y_{1},\dots,G_{n}/y_{n}]\in\mathfrak{C}(\{Q[G_{1}/y_{1},\dots,G_{n}/y_{n}]\})
by Lemma
59
.

Lemma 70
.

If
\mathfrak{C}
satisfies
\mathsf{SS}
, then for any type
\sigma
, for all
\mathfrak{C}
-interpretations
\mathcal{M}_{1},\mathcal{M}_{2}
and
\mathfrak{C}
-closed relation assignment
R
over
\mathcal{M}_{1},\mathcal{M}_{2}
,
|\Gamma^{\infty}\vdash\sigma|_{\mathcal{M}_{i}}^{\mathfrak{C}}
, for
i=1,2
, is
\mathfrak{C}
-closed and
\langle\Gamma^{\infty}\vdash\sigma\rangle^{\mathfrak{C}}_{R}
is a
\mathfrak{C}
-closed relation.

Proof 5.12
.

Given a
\mathfrak{C}
-interpretation
\mathcal{M}
, to prove that
|\Gamma^{\infty}\vdash\sigma|_{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathfrak{C}}
is
\mathfrak{C}
-closed it suffices to show that for all
P\in|\Gamma^{\infty}\vdash\sigma|_{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathfrak{C}}
,
\mathfrak{C}(\{P\})\subseteq|\Gamma^{\infty}\vdash\sigma|_{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathfrak{C}}
(by
\mathsf{SU}
and Proposition
21
).
|\Gamma^{\infty}\vdash\sigma|_{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathfrak{C}}
contains all
P
such that
P[Q_{1}/x_{i_{1}},\dots,Q_{n}/x_{i_{n}}]\in|\sigma|_{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathfrak{C}}
, for all
Q_{j}\in|\tau_{i_{j}}|_{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathfrak{C}}
, given
\mathtt{FV}(P)=\{x_{i_{1}},\dots,x_{i_{n}}\}
.
If
\mathfrak{C}
is
\mathsf{SS}
, then for all
P^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{C}\{P\}
,
P^{\prime}[Q_{1}/x_{i_{1}},\dots,Q_{n}/x_{i_{n}}]\in\mathfrak{C}\{P[Q_{1}/x_{i_{1}},\dots,Q_{n}/x_{i_{n}}]\}\subseteq\mathfrak{C}(|\sigma|^{\mathfrak{C}}_{\mathcal{M}})=|\sigma|^{\mathfrak{C}}_{\mathcal{M}}
.
Hence

P^{\prime}[Q_{1}/x_{i_{1}},\dots,Q_{n}/x_{i_{n}}]\in|\sigma|_{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathfrak{C}}
, from which we deduce
P^{\prime}\in|\Gamma^{\infty}\vdash\sigma|_{\mathcal{M}}
. We conclude that
|\Gamma^{\infty}\vdash\sigma|_{\mathcal{M}}
is
\mathfrak{C}
-closed.

Suppose now
P\in|\Gamma^{\infty}\vdash\sigma|_{\mathcal{M}_{1}}^{\mathfrak{C}},Q\in|\Gamma^{\infty}\vdash\sigma|_{\mathcal{M}_{2}}^{\mathfrak{C}}
,
P\ \langle\Gamma^{\infty}\vdash\sigma\rangle^{\mathfrak{C}}_{R}\ Q
and
P^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{C}(\{P\}),Q^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{C}(\{Q\})
. Since the sets
|\Gamma^{\infty}\vdash\sigma|_{\mathcal{M}_{i}}^{\mathfrak{C}}
are
\mathfrak{C}
-closed,
P^{\prime}\in|\Gamma^{\infty}\vdash\sigma|_{\mathcal{M}_{1}}^{\mathfrak{C}},Q^{\prime}\in|\Gamma^{\infty}\vdash\sigma|_{\mathcal{M}_{2}}^{\mathfrak{C}}
. Let now
\mathtt{FV}(P)\cup\mathtt{FV}(Q)=\{x_{i_{1}},\dots,x_{i_{n}}\}
,
F_{j}\in|\tau_{i_{j}}|_{\mathcal{M}_{1}}^{\mathfrak{C}}
,
G_{j}\in|\tau_{i_{j}}|_{\mathcal{M}_{2}}^{\mathfrak{C}}
and
F_{j}\ R[\tau_{i_{j}}]\ G_{j}
. Then
P[F_{1}/x_{i_{1}},\dots,F_{n}/x_{i_{n}}]\ \langle\sigma\rangle^{\mathfrak{C}}_{R}\ Q[G_{1}/x_{i_{1}},\dots,G_{n}/x_{i_{n}}]
and since
\langle\sigma\rangle^{\mathfrak{C}}_{R}
is a
\mathfrak{C}
-closed relation, by Lemma
69
,
P^{\prime}[F_{1}/x_{i_{1}},\dots,F_{n}/x_{i_{n}}]\ \langle\sigma\rangle^{\mathfrak{C}}_{R}\ Q^{\prime}[G_{1}/x_{i_{1}},\dots,G_{n}/x_{i_{n}}]
, hence
P^{\prime}\ \langle\Gamma^{\infty}\vdash\sigma\rangle^{\mathfrak{C}}_{R}\ Q^{\prime}
, which proves that
\langle\Gamma^{\infty}\vdash\sigma\rangle^{\mathfrak{C}}_{R}
is a
\mathfrak{C}
-closed relation.

We can now prove the
\mathfrak{C}
-invariance theorem:

Theorem 71
.

Let
\mathfrak{C}
be an adequate
\mathsf{F}
-closure operator over
L\subseteq\Lambda
satisfying
\mathsf{SU}
,
\mathsf{SA}
and
\mathsf{SS}
. Then for any type
\sigma
and closed term
M
, if
M\in\|\sigma\|^{\mathfrak{C}}
, then
M
is
\mathfrak{C}
-invariant at
\sigma
.

Proof 5.13
.

Let
\mathcal{M}_{\mathtt{p}}:\mathtt{TypeVar}\to S_{\mathfrak{C}}
be the assignment such that, for all
X\in\mathtt{TypeVar}
,
\mathcal{M}_{\mathtt{p}}(X)
is the set of all terms
M\in\bigcap_{\mathcal{M}:\mathtt{TypeVar}\to S_{\mathfrak{C}}}|\Gamma^{\infty}\vdash X|_{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathfrak{C}}
such that, for all
\mathcal{M}_{1},\mathcal{M}_{2}:\mathtt{TypeVar}\to S_{\mathfrak{C}}
and
\mathfrak{C}
-closed relation assignment
R
over
\mathcal{M}_{1},\mathcal{M}_{2}
,
P\ \langle\Gamma^{\infty}\vdash X\rangle^{\mathfrak{C}}_{R}\ P
.
\mathcal{M}_{\mathtt{p}}(X)
is a
\mathfrak{C}
-closed set:
\bigcap_{\mathcal{M}:\mathtt{TypeVar}\to S_{\mathfrak{C}}}|\Gamma^{\infty}\vdash X|_{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathfrak{C}}
is a
\mathfrak{C}
-closed set, as it is the intersection of a non-empty family of
\mathfrak{C}
-closed sets (by Lemma
70
); since for all
\mathfrak{C}
-interpretations
\mathcal{M}_{1},\mathcal{M}_{2}
and
\mathfrak{C}
-closed relation assignment
R
over them,
\langle\Gamma^{\infty}\vdash X\rangle^{\mathfrak{C}}_{R}
is a
\mathfrak{C}
-closed relation (by Lemma
70
), if
P\ \langle\Gamma^{\infty}\vdash X\rangle^{\mathfrak{C}}_{R}\ P
and
P^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{C}(\{P\})
, then
P^{\prime}\ \langle\Gamma^{\infty}\vdash X\rangle^{\mathfrak{C}}_{R}\ P^{\prime}
, hence the set of terms such that
P\ \langle\Gamma^{\infty}\vdash X\rangle^{\mathfrak{C}}_{R}\ P
contains all
\mathfrak{C}
-closures of its singletons and, as
\mathfrak{C}
satisfies
\mathsf{SU}
, is
\mathfrak{C}
-closed. It follows that
\mathcal{M}_{\mathtt{p}}(X)
is the intersection of a non-empty family of
\mathfrak{C}
-closed sets, and is thus
\mathfrak{C}
-closed.

We claim that, for any type
\sigma
, the following hold:

(1)

P\in|\sigma|_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathtt{p}}}^{\mathfrak{C}}
iff, for any
\mathcal{M}_{1},\mathcal{M}_{2}:\mathtt{TypeVar}\to S_{\mathfrak{C}}
and
\mathfrak{C}
-closed relation assignment
R
over them,
P\ \langle\Gamma^{\infty}\vdash\sigma\rangle^{\mathfrak{C}}_{R}\ P
;

(2)

for every variable
x_{i}
such that
\tau_{i}=\sigma
,
x_{i}\in|\sigma|_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathtt{p}}}^{\mathfrak{C}}
.

If for all
\mathcal{M}_{1},\mathcal{M}_{2}:\mathtt{TypeVar}\to S_{\mathfrak{C}}
and
\mathfrak{C}
-closed relation assignment
R
over them,

P\ \langle\Gamma^{\infty}\vdash\sigma\rangle^{\mathfrak{C}}_{R}\ P
holds, then, by definition,
P\in|\sigma|_{\mathcal{M}_{1}}^{\mathfrak{C}}\cap|\sigma|_{\mathcal{M}_{2}}^{\mathfrak{C}}
, hence in definitive
P\in\|\sigma\|^{\mathfrak{C}}\subseteq|\sigma|_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathtt{p}}}^{\mathfrak{C}}
. Hence the “if” direction of Claim 1. holds.
We argue for the “only if” direction of
1.
and for
2.
by a simultaneous induction on
\sigma
.

If
\sigma=X
, then, by definition of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathtt{p}}
, any
P\in|\sigma|_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathtt{p}}}^{\mathfrak{C}}
is such that, for any
\mathcal{M}_{1},\mathcal{M}_{2}:\mathtt{TypeVar}\to S_{\mathfrak{C}}
and
\mathfrak{C}
-closed relation assignment
R
over them,
P\ \langle\Gamma^{\infty}\vdash\sigma\rangle^{\mathfrak{C}}_{R}\ P
holds, so claim
1.
holds; moreover, if
\tau_{i}=X
, then, for any
\mathcal{M}_{1},\mathcal{M}_{2}:\mathtt{TypeVar}\to S_{\mathfrak{C}}
and
\mathfrak{C}
-closed relation assignment
R
over
\mathcal{M}_{1},\mathcal{M}_{2}
,
x_{i}\ \langle\Gamma^{\infty}\vdash X\rangle^{\mathfrak{C}}_{R}\ x_{i}
: if
F\in\mathcal{M}_{1}(X),G\in\mathcal{M}_{2}(X)
and
F\ R[X]\ G
, then
x_{i}[F/x_{i}]=F\ R[X]\ G=x_{i}[G/x_{i}]
. Hence claim
2.
holds too.

Let now
\sigma=\sigma_{1}\to\sigma_{2}
. By induction hypothesis, for all
i
, if
\tau_{i}=\sigma_{1}
then
x_{i}\in|\sigma_{1}|_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathtt{p}}}^{\mathfrak{C}}
; let then
P\in|\sigma|_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathtt{p}}}^{\mathfrak{C}}
and choose an index
i
such that
\tau_{i}=\sigma_{1}
and
x_{i}
does not occur free in
P
; we have that
Px_{i}\in|\sigma_{2}|_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathtt{p}}}^{\mathfrak{C}}
hence, by induction hypothesis, for any
\mathcal{M}_{1},\mathcal{M}_{2}:\mathtt{TypeVar}\to S_{\mathfrak{C}}
and
\mathfrak{C}
-closed relation assignment
R
over them,
(Px_{i})\ \langle\Gamma^{\infty}\vdash\sigma_{2}\rangle^{\mathfrak{C}}_{R}\ (Px_{i})
; let then
\mathcal{M}_{1},\mathcal{M}_{2}:\mathtt{TypeVar}\to S_{\mathfrak{C}}
and
R
be a
\mathfrak{C}
-closed relation assignment over them; by letting
\mathtt{FV}(P)=\{x_{i_{1}},\dots,x_{i_{n}}\}
, suppose
F_{j}\in|\tau_{i_{j}}|_{\mathcal{M}_{1}}^{\mathfrak{C}},G_{j}\in|\tau_{i_{j}}|_{\mathcal{M}_{2}}^{\mathfrak{C}}
are such that
F_{j}\ \langle\tau_{i_{j}}\rangle^{\mathfrak{C}}_{R}\ G_{j}
, for
1\leq j\leq n
, and moreover suppose
F\in|\sigma_{1}|_{\mathcal{M}_{1}}^{\mathfrak{C}},G\in|\sigma_{1}|_{\mathcal{M}_{2}}^{\mathfrak{C}}
are such that
F\ \langle\sigma_{1}\rangle^{\mathfrak{C}}_{R}\ G
; then, since
\mathtt{FV}(Px_{i})=\{x_{i_{1}},\dots,x_{i_{n}},x_{i}\}
(observe that
x_{i}\neq x_{i_{1}},\dots,x_{i_{n}}
as
x_{i}
has been chosen not to occur free in
P
), we have

\begin{split}P[F_{1}/x_{i_{1}},\dots,F_{n}/x_{i_{n}}]F\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad&\\
\ =\ (Px_{i})[F_{1}/x_{i_{1}},\dots,F_{n}/x_{i_{n}},F/x_{i}]\ &\langle\sigma_{2}\rangle^{\mathfrak{C}}_{R}\ (Px_{i})[G_{1}/x_{i_{1}},\dots,G_{n}/x_{i_{n}},G/x_{i}]\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\ =\ P[G_{1}/x_{i_{1}},\dots,G_{n}/x_{i_{n}}]G\end{split}

(11)

so we conclude that
P\ \langle\Gamma^{\infty}\vdash\sigma\rangle^{\mathfrak{C}}_{R}\ P
, and we proved claim
1
.

To prove claim
2.
, suppose
x_{i}
is a variable such that
\tau_{i}=\sigma
. Let
\sigma=\sigma_{1}\to\sigma_{2}
and
Q\in|\sigma_{1}|_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathtt{p}}}^{\mathfrak{C}}
; by induction hypothesis, for all
\mathcal{M}_{1},\mathcal{M}_{2}:\mathtt{TypeVar}\to S_{\mathfrak{C}}
and
\mathfrak{C}
-closed relation assignment
R
over
\mathcal{M}_{1},\mathcal{M}_{2}
,
Q\ \langle\Gamma^{\infty}\vdash\sigma_{1}\rangle^{\mathfrak{C}}_{R}\ Q
. Let
\mathcal{M}_{1},\mathcal{M}_{2}:\mathtt{TypeVar}\to S_{\mathfrak{C}}
, and
R
be a
\mathfrak{C}
-closed relation assignment over
\mathcal{M}_{1},\mathcal{M}_{2}
; moreover let

\mathtt{FV}(Q)\cup\{x_{i}\}
be the set
\{x_{i_{1}},\dots,x_{i_{r}}\}
, where
i=i_{p}
, for some
1\leq p\leq r
. Given terms
F_{1},G_{1},\dots,F_{r},G_{r}
such that
F_{j}\in|\tau_{i_{j}}|^{\mathfrak{C}}_{\mathcal{M}_{1}},G_{j}\in|\tau_{i_{j}}|^{\mathfrak{C}}_{\mathcal{M}_{2}}
and
F_{j}\ \langle\tau_{i_{j}}\rangle^{\mathfrak{C}}_{R}\ G_{j}
all hold for
1\leq j\leq r
, we have that

(x_{i}Q)[F_{1}/x_{i_{1}},\dots,F_{r}/x_{i_{r}}]\ =\ F_{i_{p}}(Q[F_{1}/x_{i_{1}},\dots,F_{r}/x_{i_{r}}])

(12)

and

(x_{i}Q)[G_{1}/x_{i_{1}},\dots,G_{r}/x_{i_{r}}]\ =\ G_{i_{p}}(Q[G_{1}/x_{i_{1}},\dots,G_{r}/x_{i_{r}}])

(13)

Since
Q[F_{1}/x_{i_{1}},\dots,F_{r}/x_{i_{r}}]\ \langle\sigma_{1}\rangle^{\mathfrak{C}}_{R}\ Q[G_{1}/x_{i_{1}},\dots,G_{r}/x_{i_{r}}]
holds by induction hypothesis and
F_{i_{p}}\ \langle\sigma\rangle^{\mathfrak{C}}_{R}\ G_{i_{p}}
, it follows that
x_{i}Q\ \langle\Gamma^{\infty}\vdash\sigma_{2}\rangle^{\mathfrak{C}}_{R}\ x_{i}Q
. We deduce that
x_{i}Q\in|\sigma_{2}|_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathtt{p}}}^{\mathfrak{C}}
, that is,
x_{i}\in|\sigma|_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathtt{p}}}^{\mathfrak{C}}
and claim
2.
is proved.

Let now
\sigma=\forall X\tau
. If
P\in|\sigma|_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathtt{p}}}^{\mathfrak{C}}
, then
P\in|\tau|^{\mathfrak{C}}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathtt{p}}(X),\mathcal{M}_{\mathtt{p}}(X_{1}),\dots,\mathcal{M}_{\mathtt{p}}(X_{q}))=|\tau|^{\mathfrak{C}}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathtt{p}}}
where
\mathtt{FV}(\tau)=\{X,X_{1},\dots,X_{q}\}
, hence, by induction hypothesis, for any two assignments
\mathcal{M}_{1},\mathcal{M}_{2}:\mathtt{TypeVar}\to S_{\mathfrak{C}}
and
\mathfrak{C}
-closed relation assignment
R
over them,
P\ \langle\Gamma^{\infty}\vdash\tau\rangle^{\mathfrak{C}}_{R}\ P
.
This implies in particular that, for any two assignments
\mathcal{M}_{1},\mathcal{M}_{2}:\mathtt{TypeVar}\to S_{\mathfrak{C}}
and
\mathfrak{C}
-closed relation assignment
R
over them,
P\ \langle\Gamma^{\infty}\vdash\forall X\tau\rangle^{\mathfrak{C}}_{R}\ P
holds, so we proved claim 1. Now let
x_{i}
be such that
\tau_{i}=\sigma
and let
\mathcal{M}_{1},\mathcal{M}_{2}:\mathtt{TypeVar}\to S_{\mathfrak{C}}
and
R
be a relation assignment over them. Let moreover
P\in|\sigma|_{\mathcal{M}_{1}}^{\mathfrak{C}},Q\in|\sigma|_{\mathcal{M}_{2}}^{\mathfrak{C}}
be such that
P\ \langle\sigma\rangle^{\mathfrak{C}}_{R}\ Q
. Then
x_{i}[P/x_{i}]=P\ \langle\sigma\rangle^{\mathfrak{C}}_{R}\ Q=x_{i}[Q/x_{i}]
, so we conclude that for all
\mathcal{M}_{1},\mathcal{M}_{2}:\mathtt{TypeVar}\to S_{\mathfrak{C}}
and
\mathfrak{C}
-closed relation assignment
R
over then,
x_{i}\ \langle\Gamma^{\infty}\vdash\sigma\rangle^{\mathfrak{C}}_{R}\ x_{i}
. From 1. we conclude tgen
x_{i}\in|\sigma|^{\mathfrak{C}}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathtt{p}}}
.

Finally, let
\sigma\in\mathtt{T}
and suppose
M
is closed and
M\in\|\sigma\|^{\mathfrak{C}}
. Then
M\in|\sigma|_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathtt{p}}}^{\mathfrak{C}}
, so, for every
\mathcal{M}_{1},\mathcal{M}_{2}:\mathtt{TypeVar}\to S_{\mathfrak{C}}
and
\mathfrak{C}
-closed relation assignment
R
over
\mathcal{M}_{1},\mathcal{M}_{2}
,
M\ \langle\Gamma^{\infty}\vdash\sigma\rangle^{\mathfrak{C}}_{R}\ M
. As
M
is closed, this implies
M\ \langle\sigma\rangle^{\mathfrak{C}}_{R}\ M
.

Theorem
71
can now be applied to all non-regular closure operators discussed in Section
3
.

Corollary 72
.

(i)

Let
\mathfrak{C}
be any among
(\_)^{\beta},(\_)^{\beta\eta},(\_)^{\beta sat}
. Then, for any type
\sigma
and closed term
M
, if
M\in\|\sigma\|^{\mathfrak{C}}
, then
M
is
\mathfrak{C}
-invariant at
\sigma
.

(ii)

Let
\mathfrak{C}
be either
\mathsf{CR}^{\prime}
or
\mathsf{CR}^{\prime\prime}
. Then, for any type
\sigma
and closed term
M
, if
M\in\|\sigma\|^{\mathfrak{C}}
, then
M
is
\beta
-invariant and
\beta\eta
-invariant at
\sigma
.

Proof 5.14
.
